| Not in the pad edit | **not ported**: padstacks (front, inner and back differing; the trapezoid, chamfered and custom shapes are section 27), fabrication property, pad number and pin function, pad-to-die length, backdrill and post-machining; the zone filler's overlay is not repeated here (next row) | `dialog_pad_properties.cpp` |
| The zone filler's overlay (`DrawingsSection::zone_overrides`, `Cmd::SetFootprintZoneConnection`, `Cmd::SetPadZoneOverrides`; section 8): a footprint's zone connection and clearance, and per pad number the pad connection, relief gap, spoke width, spoke angle and clearance | **done by the zone filler** (GAPS.md item 13); it sits beside `footprint_edits` and the two are never repeated: a pad has one clearance, the overlay's. The same dialogs below edit both | `FOOTPRINT::SetLocalZoneConnection`, `SetLocalClearance`, `PAD::SetLocalZoneConnection`, `SetLocalThermalGapOverride`, `SetLocalThermalSpokeWidthOverride`, `SetThermalSpokeAngle` |
| The Footprint Properties dialog: the field grid (Name, Value, Show, Width, Height, Thickness, Layer, and with "Show all columns" the italic, bold, mirrored, keep upright, knockout, justification and offset), Add Field and Delete Field, placement (position, orientation, side, locked), component type and the attributes, and the zone connection fields of the Clearances tab and of Pad Properties (the footprint's connection and clearance; per pad number the pad connection, relief gap, spoke width, spoke angle and clearance); one OK is one undo step (`edit_board_footprint`, then the move, turn, flip and lock, then `set_footprint_zone_connection` and `set_pad_zone_overrides` for what was changed) | **done**; orientation is KiCad's counter-clockwise (the model's `rot` runs clockwise); a position lands on the placement grid like every pose | `DIALOG_FOOTPRINT_PROPERTIES::TransferDataFromWindow`, `Validate` |
| The Pad Properties dialog (a double click, or Properties on a pad): type, shape, size, offset, rotation, corner radius, hole, the mask and paste overrides (an override that is switched off puts the library's value back) and the zone filler's fields of the same panel (the clearance, pad connection, relief gap, spoke width and angle: those of every pad that carries the number); one OK is one undo step (`edit_board_pad` and `set_pad_zone_overrides` in one batch); the checks are `PAD::CheckPad`'s ("Error: (Pad must have a positive size)", "Through hole pad has no hole") | **done** | `DIALOG_PAD_PROPERTIES::TransferDataFromWindow`, `padValuesOK` |
| The Properties panel: the rows of a `PCB_FIELD`, the footprint's attribute rows and the pad's type, shape, size, corner radius and hole rows (section 23) | **done** | `PCB_FIELD_DESC`, `FOOTPRINT_DESC`, `PAD_DESC` |
| The `.kicad_pcb`: `(property "Name" "text" (at x y a) (layer ...) [(hide yes)] (effects ...))` for every field (bottom-side fields in the file's mirrored frame), `(attr ...)`, the pad offset inside `(drill ...)`, the margins; read back by the importer into the same `FootprintEdit` (the old `fp_text` form too, and a reference shared with another footprint keeps the text the file has). One `(pad ..)` form carries the pad edit's extras and the zone filler's fields (`clearance`, `zone_connect`, `thermal_bridge_width`, `thermal_bridge_angle`, `thermal_gap`), in KiCad's order, and each half is read back into its own overlay | **done**; kicad-cli loads it | `format( FOOTPRINT )`, `parseFOOTPRINT_unchecked` -- `crates/kicad/src/fp_fields.rs`, `pcb.rs`, `import.rs` |
| Duplicate, Copy, Paste and Create Array take a footprint's edit and its zone overrides with them (a copy under its new reference); Rip up removes the edit and the zone overrides; the clipboard text carries both (its one `(pad ..)` form has the extras and the zone fields), and a paste of a footprint the model knows by name keeps the pads that are not the library's (a shape, size or hole edited where it was copied) as the copy's pad edits | **done** | `kicad_clipboard.cpp` -- `ClipFootprint.edit`, `ClipFootprint.zone_overrides`, `pad_edits_between` |
| Change Footprint(s), Update Footprints from Library, geographical reannotate | **done**, section 30 | `dialog_exchange_footprints.cpp`, `dialog_update_pcb.cpp`, `board_reannotate_tool.cpp` |

Rust (69 new tests): `crates/model/src/fp_edit.rs` (16: the frames, the defaults, pad lookup, hole edits, derived attributes, JSON, the pad edits between two footprints), `crates/ops/src/fp_edit_tests.rs` (11: the verbs and their refusals, the symbol sync,
fields that move, turn and flip on their own and with their footprint), `crates/kicad/src/fp_fields.rs` and `pcb.rs` (13: the text written and read back on either side, the old form, the attributes in KiCad's order,
a shared reference, one pad form with both overlays), `crates/ops/src/array_tests.rs` (24, section 17) and five in `crates/cli/src/board.rs` (an edit is one undo step and reaches the model and the file; a duplicate keeps it and the
zone overrides; a copy and paste keeps both, here and on another board; an array is one undo step; **slow tier**: `kicad_cli_loads_the_edited_board_and_its_position_and_bom_exports_honour_the_attributes` runs real kicad-cli on the written board). Web (39 new `node --test` cases): `kicad-port/fpFields.ts`
(ids, layouts, commands, the checks), `pcbProperties.ts` (the new rows), `arrayOptions.ts`, `padZone.ts` (the commands the dialogs' OK sends for the zone fields); the painter, hit testing and carry draw, pick and move a field.

Verified in the browser (port 8809, a scratch board made with `eda board new examples/mcu_board_30plus.yaml` and eight footprints placed with `eda board place`; the page's pointer events were dispatched in the page, the real
pointer never touched): `e2e/footprint-edit.check.js` runs 19 scenarios through `window.__eda`, each undone and the board compared with how it was -- a field selected as an item, moved with Move Exactly, carried with M and
dragged by the pointer (one undo step, its footprint not moved); the Appearance panel's References, Values and Footprint Text rows hiding fields, and a hidden field not picked; the panel edits of a field (visible, thickness, justification, layer) and of a footprint (do not populate, exclude from BOM and from position files, component type); the Footprint Properties
dialog (a reference size, a new user field, an orientation and an attribute in one OK, one undo; a user field added, renamed and deleted; a reserved name refused with nothing sent; E on a field opens it on that field's row); Flip (fields on the
back layers, mirrored); the Footprint Properties dialog's zone connection fields beside the field grid and the attributes (one OK, one undo for all of it); the Pad Properties dialog (shape, size, offset, clearance and the zone connection in one OK, one undo; a pad with no size refused in the check's words); Copy and Paste of a footprint with a pad edit and a zone override (the paste has both); Create Array as a 3 x 2 grid with unique references, four
footprints on a circle with "Full circle", and Arrange selection, each one undo step, the dialog's remembered values, the messages of bad entries and Escape. With the real kicad-cli on the scratch board after edits sent to
`/api/cmd`: `/api/fab/pos` with `exclude_dnp` left out the footprint marked do not populate and the one excluded from position files, and `/api/fab/bom` left the do-not-populate one out of the BOM.
A hidden Browser pane stops the page's timers, which `__eda.run` waits on: the script then drives them from a fetch loop (its header says how to read a run that outlasts a tool call).

**Approximations, and what is left.**
- A footprint's position lands on the placement grid like every pose (`Board::set_pose`); the field positions do not (they are the dialog's numbers).
- The pad offset and the margins are written to the board file and read by kicad-cli, and drawn by the canvas (offset), but our own placement and courtyard checks read the pad as the engine model has it (type, shape,
  size, hole, rotation, ratio).
- A field's text is literal: KiCad's text variables are not expanded, and the Reference and Value text is the schematic's.
- Per-footprint mask and paste margin overrides, padstacks, Datasheet and Description fields and the footprint
  editor's array of pads (section 17) are left. (Change Footprint, Update from Library and geographical reannotate were left here until section 30.) (Trapezoid, chamfered and custom pads were left here until section 27.)
- The zone filler's fields address a pad by its number: the zone fields of a pad that shares its number with others are those of all of them, as the overlay keeps them.

## 27. Trapezoid, chamfered and custom pads (GAPS.md items 7, 9, 13 and 19; 2026-10-10)

Before this section the footprint IR had four pad shapes and the importer turned the three others into the nearest one: a trapezoid into its rectangle, a chamfered rectangle into a rounded one and a custom pad into its
anchor, so the gate and the router measured a rectangle, the zone filler knocked out a box and `stonehenge`'s ring pads were discs. Now a pad is the copper KiCad's is. Ported from `pcbnew/pad.cpp` (`PAD::buildEffectiveShape`,
`TransformShapeToPolygon`, `MergePrimitivesAsPolygon`, `Flip`, `CheckPad` / `doCheckPad`), `padstack.cpp`, `common/eda_shape.cpp` (`makeEffectiveShapes`), `libs/kimath/src/convert_basic_shapes_to_polygon.cpp`
(`TransformRoundChamferedRectToPolygon`), `pcb_io/kicad_sexpr/pcb_io_kicad_sexpr.cpp` and `_parser.cpp` (`format( const PAD* )`, `parsePAD`, `parsePAD_option`), `zone_filler.cpp` (`addKnockout`, `buildThermalSpokes`) and
`dialogs/dialog_pad_properties.cpp` at `8303b2ad`. The IR change is additive (`Pad::ext: PadShapeExt`, the three `PadShape` variants, `LibraryPad::custom`, new `PadEdit` fields, all with serde defaults: a design that has none of
them is byte for byte what it was), every edit is an undoable `/api/cmd` verb (`edit_board_pad`, `edit_pad`) and kicad-cli is still the only engine that judges the board.

| Behavior | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| The IR: a trapezoid's delta (one of the two is zero), a chamfered rectangle's ratio and corner flags (a chamfered pad with a corner ratio is KiCad's "chamfered with other corners rounded"), a custom pad's anchor (circle or rectangle of the pad's size), zone knockout (outline or convex hull) and basic shapes (line, arc, circle, rectangle, polygon, bezier; filled or not; a proxy item `gr_vector` / `gr_bbox` is kept and is not copper), and the shape's offset | **done** | `PAD_SHAPE::TRAPEZOID`, `CHAMFERED_RECT`, `CUSTOM`, `PADSTACK` (the single-layer properties), `PAD::GetAnchorPadShape`, `GetCustomShapeInZoneOpt` -- `crates/model/src/footprint.rs`, `ir.rs` |
| The `.kicad_pcb` and `.kicad_mod` reader and writer: `rect_delta`, `roundrect_rratio`, `chamfer_ratio`, `chamfer` (a chamfered rectangle is written as `roundrect` plus the chamfer tokens), the offset inside `drill`, `options` (clearance `outline` or `convexhull`, anchor `circle` or `rect`), `primitives` with `gr_line`, `gr_arc`, `gr_circle`, `gr_rect`, `gr_poly`, `gr_curve`, their `width` and `fill`, `gr_bbox`, `gr_vector`; a bottom-side footprint is in the file's mirrored frame (x of every position, offset, delta and primitive negated, the chamfer's corners swapped) and the two readers and the two writers apply the same mirror | **done**; a board with every shape round-trips token for token, 16 custom pads of `issue5093` and the QA boards' keep their primitives, and kicad-cli's SVG plot of the original and of our export is identical, for a board and for a library footprint (slow tier) | `parsePAD`, `parsePAD_option`, `format( const PAD* )`, `PAD::Flip` -- `crates/kicad/src/pad_shape.rs` (the one place the four go through), `pcb.rs`, `import.rs`, `footprint_lib.rs`, `footprint_import.rs` |
| The copper: a trapezoid is its four corners, a chamfered rectangle is `TransformRoundChamferedRectToPolygon` (the corners cut, the others rounded by the ratio, 16 segments to a quarter as KiCad), a custom pad is its anchor and every primitive at its width (a line is a stadium, an arc and a ring strokes, a filled shape its area and half its width all round). One neutral list of pieces (`PadPiece`) carried to the board by the pad's rotation, the footprint's side and pose | **done**; areas against kicad-cli's plot: the two custom pads of `custom_pads.kicad_pcb` within 0.1%, `stonehenge`'s ring (a stroked circle) within 0.3%, the 16 custom pads of `issue5093` within 0.2%; a chamfered rectangle's rounded corners use KiCad's own segment count | `PAD::buildEffectiveShape`, `EDA_SHAPE::makeEffectiveShapes` -- `crates/model/src/pad_geom.rs` |
| The DRC, the routing clearance gate, the router's solids and its shove hulls, connectivity and the placement checks measure that copper (`DrcPad`, `PlacedPad::pieces`, `eda_drc::pad_shape`); the router hulls a custom pad part by part | **done**; the gap to a trapezoid's slanted side, a chamfered corner and a custom pad's arm is within 2 um of the `actual` in kicad-cli's DRC report (slow tier) | `PAD::GetEffectiveShape`, `DRC_ENGINE` -- `crates/drc/src/{pad_shape,board,kimath}.rs`, `crates/gates/src/pcb.rs`, `crates/pns/src/{hull,shove}.rs` |
| The zone fill (kicad-cli's since 2026-10-13; the Rust filler's before) knocks out, clears and relieves the real shape: a custom pad is a compound, a ring is relieved inside and out, `(clearance convexhull)` knocks out the hull, the spokes start from the copper's box (mirrored for a bottom-side footprint) | **done**; `stonehenge` GND 11.33% to 0.15% of kicad-cli's area, `issue5093` `/right/GND` 776.56% to 1.49% (`crates/zone-filler/PARITY.md` (deleted 2026-10-13: `git show 43f6c09:crates/zone-filler/PARITY.md`)) | `ZONE_FILLER::addKnockout`, `buildThermalSpokes` -- `crates/zone-filler/src/{shape,lib,spokes,islands}.rs` |
| The board canvas draws the copper's outline (state `pads[].outline`, contours in micrometres filled even-odd, so a ring has its hole), and a click and a snap find the pad by those contours, not by its box (`padOutline.ts`); the footprint editor draws a custom pad's anchor and shapes (a selected pad has a halo round the union), picks it by them and frames them (zoom to fit, the chooser preview, box select) | **done**; a footprint editor click on the arm of a custom pad selects it | `PCB_PAINTER::draw( const PAD* )`, `PAD::HitTest` -- `painter.ts`, `footprintPainter.ts`, `libEditorHit.ts`, `itemBoxes.ts` |
| 3D and Gerber: the Gerber, drill and 3D exports are kicad-cli's, which reads our files, so they carry the real shapes with nothing more to do (checked: `pcb export svg` of our export draws the same pads, and `pcb export gerbers` of a board with the three shapes writes the trapezoid as a 4-point outline aperture with KiCad's corners and the chamfered and the custom pad as free polygons); the instant 3D scene can take the outline from `eda_drc::pad_shape::pad_outline` (polygons, each outline then its holes) or `pads[].outline` of the state | **done** for the exports; **exposed, not wired** in the instant scene (the 3D viewer's files were not touched) | `PAD::TransformShapeToPolygon` |
| The Pad Properties dialog (the board's and the footprint editor's, one view of the shape: `PadShapeFields.tsx`): the nine shape choices in KiCad's words and order (circular, oval, rectangular, trapezoidal, rounded rectangle, chamfered rectangle, chamfered with other corners rounded, custom with a circular or a rectangular base), what choosing one sets up (a chamfered rectangle's 20% chamfer with its top-left corner cut, the IPC corner ratio of a rounded one, the spoke angle 45 degrees for a circle and 90 after it), the trapezoid's axis and delta, the chamfer's ratio and four corners (the ones a ratio allows), the zone knockout of a custom pad, and the list of basic shapes: add (a line, arc, circle, rectangle, polygon or bezier drawn over the pad), edit (its points, radius, width, fill; a polygon's points added and deleted), duplicate, delete; what OK sends is one undoable edit | **done**; the limits are `getMaxChamferRatio` and `getMaxCornerRadius` | `DIALOG_PAD_PROPERTIES::onPadShapeSelection`, `updateAllowedPadChamferCorners`, `transferDataToPad`, `dialog_shape_properties.cpp` (the fields of a basic shape) -- `kicad-port/padShape.ts` |
| The checks: "Error: (trapezoid delta is too large)", "(negative corner chamfer is not allowed)", "(corner chamfer is too large)", a basic shape that cannot be drawn (a polygon of two points, a circle with no radius, a line with no length, an arc through two equal points, a negative width), and "The custom pad shape must resolve to a single polygon." when the anchor and the shapes make two pieces of copper | **done**, in the dialog and again in the verb | `PAD::CheckPad`, `doCheckPad`, `padValuesOK` -- `crates/ops/src/fp_edit.rs`, `crates/model/src/footprint.rs::validate` |
| Pad Table has "Custom shape" as `DIALOG_FP_EDIT_PAD_TABLE` does; Push Pad Properties and Paste Pad Properties carry the custom shapes with the rest of the settings; a footprint's pad edits (`FootprintEdit::pads`) hold the new fields, and a footprint pasted on the board keeps them | **done** | `PAD::ImportSettingsFrom`, `dialog_fp_edit_pad_table.cpp` -- `library_editors.rs::import_pad_settings`, `fp_edit.rs::pad_edits_between` |

Rust (40 new tests): `crates/model/src/pad_geom.rs` (8: the pieces and the polygons, the frame, the default spoke angle, the distance to each piece), `crates/drc/src/{pad_shape,kimath}.rs` (7: a trapezoid's area and
bounding box, a ring's hole, the hull of a compound, a compound's gap), `crates/gates/src/pcb.rs` (3: the gate measures a trapezoid's slant, a chamfered corner and a custom pad's arm), `crates/pns/src/hull.rs` (1: a custom pad's hull
part by part), `crates/kicad/src/pad_shape.rs` and `footprint_lib.rs` (4: the tokens read and written, the mirror), `crates/kicad/tests/pad_shapes.rs` (9: a board with every shape round-trips, a bottom-side footprint is mirrored as `PAD::Flip` does, the
QA boards' custom pads keep their primitives, areas against kicad-cli; **slow tier**: kicad-cli's plot of the original and of the export of a board and of a library footprint is identical, and its DRC measures the clearances the DRC here does),
`crates/ops/src/fp_edit_tests.rs` (2: the new edit fields, the refusals) and `library_editors.rs` (1: Push and Paste Pad Properties carry the shapes), `crates/zone-filler/tests/basic.rs` (3, deleted 2026-10-13 with the filler) and, **slow tier**, `kicad_cli_parity.rs` (2: `stonehenge` GND, `issue5093` `/right/GND`).
Web (25 new `node --test` cases): `kicad-port/padShape.ts` (the choices, what choosing one sets, the limits, the commands, the basic shapes' fields and checks, a trapezoid's corners, the extent of a custom pad), `libEditorHit.ts` (a click on a custom pad in the footprint editor) and `padOutline.ts` (a point on the copper of a ring pad, and the distance to it).

Verified in the browser (port 8816, a scratch board made with `eda board new examples/mcu_board_30plus.yaml` and three footprints placed with `eda board place`; the real pointer never touched): `e2e/pad-shapes.check.js` runs 6 scenarios
through `window.__eda`, each undone and the board compared with how it was -- the dialog's nine choices; a rectangle made a trapezoid (its delta, the copper in the state, one undo); a chamfered rectangle with other corners rounded (the
defaults, the limits, the cut corners); a custom pad with a line, a ring and a polygon added, edited and deleted (the copper is their union); the Properties panel's seven shapes; and no console error. In the footprint editor a new pad
made custom (circular base) with a line, an arc and a polygon is drawn as its union, and a click on the arm selects the pad; on the board a pad made custom with a line is selected by a click on its arm and not by one in the empty corner of its box. With kicad-cli: the Gerber export of a board with the three shapes (above).

**Approximations, and what is left.**
- A pad has one shape for all its layers: a `(padstack ..)` with different front, inner and back shapes is read as its front shape, and `remove_unused_layers` (no copper on the inner layers of a plated hole) is not kept.
- The proxy spokes of a custom pad (`gr_vector`) are kept and written back but not used: its thermal spokes come from the box of its copper, not along the proxy (`crates/zone-filler/PARITY.md` (deleted 2026-10-13: `git show 43f6c09:crates/zone-filler/PARITY.md`)).
- A `gr_rect` primitive's corner radius and the arcs inside a `gr_poly` primitive are not in the IR.
- Explode and Recombine (`Ctrl+E`) are not written (above); the footprint editor's pick of a trapezoid or a chamfered pad is its box (`libEditorHit.ts`, an approximation the click does not notice).
- The instant 3D scene still draws the pad boxes it drew; `pad_outline` and the state's `outline` give it the shapes.
- Teardrops on a pad that is not round are still not ported (section 15).

## 28. Footprint graphics: the silkscreen, fab and assembly drawings and the texts of a footprint (GAPS.md items 25 and 2; 2026-10-10)

Before this section a footprint had pads, a courtyard, 3D models and fields, and none of the rest of a `.kicad_mod`: the silkscreen outline, the pin-1 mark, the fab body, the assembly drawings and the `${REFERENCE}` on `F.Fab`. A board
made here had no part outlines on screen, in the file, in the silkscreen kicad-cli plots or in the 3D view; a board imported from KiCad kept them in board space in `drawings.footprint_extras`, where only the in-house DRC read them,
and never wrote them back (`complex_hierarchy`: 512 `fp_line`s, kicad-cli's `silk_over_copper` 104 -> 2 and `silk_overlap` 199 -> 5 after our export). Now they are the footprint's own, in its frame, and a move, a turn or a flip of the footprint
carries them. Ported from `pcbnew/footprint.cpp` (`FOOTPRINT::Add`, `Flip`, `GetBoundingBox( false )`, `HitTest`), `pcb_shape.cpp` and `common/eda_shape.cpp` (`PCB_SHAPE::Flip`, `ViewGetLOD`, `STROKE_PARAMS`), `pcb_text.cpp` and
`common/eda_text.cpp` (`PCB_TEXT::Flip`, `GetDrawRotation`, `ViewGetLOD`), `pcb_io/kicad_sexpr/pcb_io_kicad_sexpr.cpp` (`format( FOOTPRINT )`, `format( PCB_SHAPE )`, `format( PCB_TEXT )`, `formatLayers`), `pcb_io_kicad_sexpr_parser.cpp`
(`parseFOOTPRINT_unchecked`, `parsePCB_SHAPE`, `parsePCB_TEXT`, `parsePCB_TEXT_effects`), `pcb_painter.cpp` (`draw( const PCB_SHAPE* )`, `draw( const PCB_TEXT* )`) and `tools/pcb_selection_tool.cpp` (`Selectable`) at `8303b2ad`.
The IR change is additive (one hook, `Footprint::art`, and `PadMaskInfo::art_layers`, both serde defaults: a design that has none is byte for byte what it was) and kicad-cli is still the only engine that judges the board.

| Behavior | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| Storage: `FpArt` (`crates/model/src/fp_art.rs`) on `Footprint::art`, in the frame of the pads (the footprint seen from the top, un-rotated, +y down), so the definition shared by every instance holds it and a move, a turn or a flip changes the pose and nothing in the art. A shape is a board `Shape` (segment, arc, circle, rectangle, polygon, Bezier curve; stroke width, solid fill) with its stroke type (solid, dash, dot, dash-dot, dash-dot-dot), a hatched fill (hatch, reverse hatch, cross hatch), a rectangle's corner radius, the layers when it is on several, and its solder mask margin; a text is a `FieldLayout` (the attributes of a field: size, thickness, layer, justification, mirror, bold, italic, keep upright, knockout) with the string, the font face and the line spacing | **done**; the types are made to be reused (`FpShape::placed`, `FpText::placed`, `FpArt::from_library` / `to_library`) | `FOOTPRINT::Add`, `PCB_SHAPE`, `EDA_SHAPE`, `PCB_TEXT`, `EDA_TEXT`, `STROKE_PARAMS` |
| Layers and sides: named as for a top-side footprint; a bottom-side instance draws every side-specific layer (copper, silkscreen, mask, paste, adhesive, courtyard, fab) on the other side, mirrors x as a pad does, and a text's angle turns the other way and its mirror flag toggles on a side-specific layer (the user layers are on both sides) | **done** | `PCB_SHAPE::Flip`, `PCB_TEXT::Flip`, `BOARD::FlipLayer`, `FOOTPRINT::Flip` |
| The reader (`crates/kicad/src/fp_art_io.rs`, for a `.kicad_mod` and for a `.kicad_pcb`): `fp_line`, `fp_arc` (also the pre-7 centre / start / angle form), `fp_circle`, `fp_rect`, `fp_poly` (an arc among its points flattened at 5 um), `fp_curve` and `fp_text user` with `effects` (size, thickness, bold, italic, justify, mirror, `unlocked`, knockout, line spacing, face), on every layer but the courtyard and the board edge (they have homes of their own: `Footprint::courtyard_outlines`, the board's Edge.Cuts shapes); a hidden text is left out (KiCad makes it a hidden field); the legacy `(property ..)` of a file older than 20230620 is not drawn unless the file says so, and the sheet and keyword properties are not fields. Two instances of one library footprint whose drawings (or pads, or models) differ are two definitions (`Lib:Name` and `Lib:Name#2`), and the writer names both `Lib:Name` | **done** | `parseFOOTPRINT_unchecked`, `parsePCB_SHAPE`, `parsePCB_TEXT`, `parsePCB_TEXT_effects` |
| The writer (`write_footprint`): after the fields and before the pads, shapes then texts as `format( FOOTPRINT )` sorts them, each with a uuid made from the footprint and the item's place; a bottom-side footprint in the file's mirrored frame (x negated, the other side's layers, a text's angle and mirror as `PCB_TEXT::Flip`); the layer lists as `formatLayers` (`"*.Cu"`, `"*.SilkS"`, the rest in layer order); the board writer and the clipboard (which writes a footprint the same way) both go through it | **done** | `format( FOOTPRINT )`, `format( PCB_SHAPE )`, `format( PCB_TEXT )`, `formatLayers` |
| The proof is kicad-cli's: its SVG plot of `F.SilkS`, `B.SilkS`, `F.Fab` and `B.Fab` of the original and of our export is the same, primitive for primitive (paths, circles, rectangles and texts, to 3 um), on six of KiCad's QA boards (`footprints_load_save`, `component_classes`, `issue24078`, `issue5102`, `issue5990`, `complex_hierarchy`: 24,090 primitives, both sides, turned footprints, texts, dashed and filled shapes), on a synthetic board with a footprint on each side, and on a board this studio made from `examples/opamp_filter.yaml` (421 fab and 175 silkscreen primitives) | **done**, slow tier (`EDA_SLOW_TESTS=1`); the boards left out hold a board-level text of several lines or of a glyph that is not square (`issue7004`, `issue8407`, `issue11814`, `issue22826`, `connect`) or a board-level dotted rectangle (`connect`), which the board-level `Text` and `Shape` do not keep | `pcb export svg` -- `crates/kicad/tests/fp_art_roundtrip.rs` |
| A part whose footprint is a built-in package (`0603`, `SOIC-8`) gets the silkscreen, fab body and texts of the installed KiCad footprint it stands for (`kicad_footprint_of_part`: `0603` on an `R` is `Resistor_SMD:R_0603_1608Metric`), fitted onto the built-in pads (`fit_pads`) and found the way the part's 3D model is (`ConstraintModel::installed_art`), so a board made from an example has the outlines of its parts; a library footprint placed by the studio brings its own (`parse_footprint_file`). The footprint editor's model keeps lines, arcs, circles, rectangles, polygons, curves and texts, and drops what it has no field for (dashes, hatches, vertical justification, bold, italic, keep upright) | **done**; best-effort when the twin is not installed | `PCB_IO_KICAD_SEXPR::FootprintLoad` -- `fp_art_io.rs::attach_installed_art`, `footprint_lib.rs` |
| Pads on the silkscreen: the through-hole pads of an older library are on `F.SilkS` too (`(layers "*.Cu" "*.Mask" "F.SilkS")`; `complex_hierarchy` has 185), and kicad-cli's silkscreen checks measure them there. The importer keeps a pad's layers besides copper, paste and mask (`PadMaskInfo::art_layers`, as for a top-side footprint) and the writer puts them back in the pad's `(layers ..)` on the footprint's side | **done** for an imported board; a library footprint's pads on the silkscreen are not read, and Pad Properties has no field for them | `PAD::GetLayerSet`, `formatLayers` |
| The board state: `parts[].graphics` (the JSON of a board `Shape`, plus `dash`, `hatch` and `layers`) and `parts[].texts` (the keys of a field, at the angle they have on the board, variables resolved), in board space like the pads, so the canvas and the hit test need no frame of their own; the part's body (the placeholder box of the 3D scene) is the box of its `F.Fab` drawings | **done** | `crates/cli/src/fp_art_json.rs`, `studio.rs` |
| The canvas: each graphic and text on its layer, back to front (user layers, fab, adhesive, paste, mask, silkscreen on top), after the pads, in the layer's colour, under the Appearance rows (the layer's switch and opacity, Footprints Front or Back, Footprint Text, and References and Values for the two variables); a filled shape takes the Filled Shapes opacity, a graphic on copper the track opacity; a dashed stroke is dashed at 11, 0.2 and 4 widths; a text is drawn a line at a time in the stroke font (mirrored, italic, bold, justified and turned as it is); a hatched fill is drawn as its outline | **done**; the hatch lines (`EDA_SHAPE::UpdateHatching`) are not drawn | `PCB_PAINTER::draw( const PCB_SHAPE* )`, `draw( const PCB_TEXT* )`, `PCB_SHAPE::ViewGetLOD`, `PCB_TEXT::ViewGetLOD` -- `components/canvas/fpArtPaint.ts`, `kicad-port/fpArt.ts` |
| Selection: the art is not an item of its own; a click on a stroke of the outline, wherever the stroke is (outside the courtyard too), picks the footprint, a hidden layer or side picks nothing, the footprint's box is its graphics' (strokes included, texts left out) with its courtyard | **done** | `PCB_SELECTION_TOOL::Selectable`, `FOOTPRINT::HitTest`, `GetBoundingBox( false )` -- `selectionCandidates.ts`, `pcbItems.ts` |
| Move, rotate and flip: the art follows the pose, so a footprint turned or flipped has its outline turned or flipped with it, its silkscreen on the other side's layer and its text mirrored; the footprint in the hand is drawn with the same transform | **done** (the actions are run in the browser check below) | `FOOTPRINT::Move`, `Rotate`, `Flip` |
| The 3D scene draws a footprint's silkscreen and its drawings on the other flat layers (adhesive, user layers) from its graphics, its texts on those layers (one line, the stroke font), under the Appearance rows of the 3D viewer (silkscreen top and bottom, adhesive, the user layers, References); the fab and courtyard layers are not drawn flat (as KiCad's 3D viewer) and the fab body is the part's placeholder box | **done** | `3d_viewer/3d_canvas/board_adapter.cpp` (the layers drawn flat) -- `viewer3d/scene.ts` (`addFootprintArt`) |
| The in-house board build (`eda_drc::DrcBoard`, which the router and connectivity read) takes a footprint's silkscreen and mask shapes, its texts and its Reference, Value and user fields from the footprint, put where it is, and its graphics on copper as netless strokes the zone keeps clear of; a board made here and one imported are read the same way (before, a board made here had an approximation of its reference label). A design imported before this still has its board-space copy in `footprint_extras` and is read from it until it is imported again. `crates/lint` has no silkscreen or mask check | **done**; kicad-cli stays the only DRC | `DRC_TEST_PROVIDER_SILK_CLEARANCE`, `ZONE_FILLER::knockoutGraphicClearance` -- `crates/drc/src/fp_art.rs` |
| `drawings.footprint_extras` keeps what is not graphics (the pads' layer sets and mask margins, the footprint's mask margin, bridges and net ties, the zone facts, the mask-only pads); the importer no longer fills its `graphics` (but for the mask-only pads) and `texts` | **done** | -- |

Rust (33 new tests): `crates/model/src/fp_art.rs` (9: the layers of each side, a shape and a text following the pose, a rectangle under a square turn and under another, the boxes, a text kept upright, the variables, the editor's conversions, empty art), `crates/kicad/src/fp_art_io.rs` (8: every shape kind
with its layer, width, style and fill, a text's effects, what is written reads back on either side, a bottom-side footprint written mirrored on the other layers and read back to the top view, the old spellings, a polygon with an arc, `formatLayers`), `crates/kicad/src/fp_fields.rs` (1: the legacy properties), `crates/kicad/tests/fp_art_roundtrip.rs` (8: a board with a footprint on each side comes back out and
reads back the same, a library footprint's silkscreen reaches the board, a board made from an example has the outlines of its parts, a pad's layers beside copper, paste and mask come back and flip with the footprint, a `#2` definition is written under its library;
**slow tier**: kicad-cli's plots equal for the synthetic board, the example board and the six QA boards), `crates/cli/src/fp_art_json.rs` (3: the state's graphics and texts on each side, the body) and `crates/drc/src/fp_art.rs` (4: silk, mask, copper and
texts put where the footprint is on each side, the fallback to the board-space copy). Web (9 new `node --test` cases): `kicad-port/fpArt.ts` (the shapes from the wire, the layers of a shape, the click on a stroke and on a filled shape, the boxes, the Appearance rows, the dash pattern, the paint order).

Verified in the browser (port 8820, a scratch board made with `eda board new examples/opamp_filter.yaml` and its parts placed with `eda board place`; the real pointer never touched): `e2e/fp-graphics.check.js` runs 6 scenarios through
`window.__eda` -- the state holds the graphics and texts of the placed footprints, the canvas draws them on their layers and in their colours (pixels read at the strokes, not at an empty corner), a click on a stroke of the outline outside the courtyard picks
the footprint, a turn and a flip carry the graphics (the silkscreen goes to the other side's layer, the text is mirrored), the 3D view draws the silkscreen, and the board is as it was after the undos with no console error. With the real kicad-cli: the
plots above, and the re-export measure below.

The measure (`tools/parity_report.py`, `REPORT.md`, section 2): on `complex_hierarchy` kicad-cli's `silk_over_copper` is 104 on the original and 104 on our export (it was 2), `silk_overlap` 199 and 199 (it was 5), `silk_edge_clearance` 14 and 14 (it was 2); on
`api_kitchen_sink` `silk_overlap` 2 and 2 (it was 0), and `component_classes` now has the same violation counts as its original, so the re-export of a real board gives kicad-cli the same counts as the original on 7 of 12 boards (it was 6). What is left on
`complex_hierarchy` is not graphics: `clearance` 112 -> 117, `copper_edge_clearance` 14 -> 15, `solder_mask_bridge` 112 -> 115, `shorting_items` 0 -> 2 and the 72 `lib_footprint_issues` of footprints that had no library (written `eda:<name>`).

**Approximations, and what is left.**
- A hatched fill (`reverse_hatch`, `cross_hatch`) is drawn as its outline, in 2D and in 3D; the hatch lines of `EDA_SHAPE::UpdateHatching` are not ported.
- The footprint editor's model has no field for a stroke type, a hatch, a vertical justification, bold, italic or keep upright, so a footprint saved from the editor loses them (the board keeps them); the editor does not draw the art of a footprint it did not make beyond what it drew before.
- The board has no verbs for a footprint's graphics: they are the library's (or the imported board's), and Rotate, Flip, Move and the pose carry them. Editing one on the board (KiCad's "Edit Footprint" in the board editor) needs the footprint editor.
- A text's variables are `${REFERENCE}`, `${VALUE}`, `${FOOTPRINT_LIBRARY}`, `${FOOTPRINT_NAME}` and `${LAYER}`; the title block's (`${TITLE}`, `${ISSUE_DATE}`) are not resolved in a footprint's text (the in-house board build used to resolve them), and a text in a font other than the stroke font is kept and drawn in the stroke font.
- Pads on the silkscreen are kept per placed footprint of an imported board (`PadMaskInfo`); a library footprint's are not read, and the 2D and 3D views do not draw a pad on a technical layer (the pad shape on the silkscreen).
- The mask-only pads (`(layers "F.Mask")` with no copper) are still not written back; they are `FootprintExtra::graphics` with a pad number.

## 29. Bringing files in: Import Graphics (DXF and SVG), Append Board and the file drops (GAPS.md item 28, package P8; `common/import_gfx/*`, `pcbnew/import_gfx/*`, `pcb_control.cpp`, `eda_base_frame.cpp`)

Before this section the only way to bring an outline or a drawing in was to redraw it, and another board could not be merged. Now File > Import > Graphics... reads a DXF or an SVG into the board's own lines, arcs,
circles, polygons, curves and text on a layer, Append Board... puts another board's items on this one, and a file dropped on the window does what its editor's `m_acceptedExts` say. The readers are hand-written (no new
dependency: KiCad uses libdxfrw and nanosvg), the files travel with the commands (like a paste's text), so every import is one `/api/cmd` verb and one undo step. Sections 5 and 25 are the paste and the outline these build on.

| Behavior | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| File > Import > Graphics... (`pcbnew.InteractiveDrawing.placeImportedGraphics`, Ctrl+Shift+F) opens the dialog; a DXF or SVG dropped on the window (`pcbnew.InteractiveDrawing.ddImportGraphics`, parameter `{ name, text }`) opens it with the file in File: | **done** (`actions/importActions.ts`, `components/ImportGraphicsDialog.tsx`, `kicad-port/importGraphics.ts`; `Cmd::ImportGraphics`, one undo step) | `DRAWING_TOOL::PlaceImportedGraphics`, `DIALOG_IMPORT_GRAPHICS`, `PCB_ACTIONS::ddImportGraphics` |
| The dialog's options: File: with Browse... (a browser file chooser; a file can be dropped on the dialog too), the server's description of the file ("8 items: 4 lines, 4 arcs", what the plugin could not handle), Import scale, DXF default line width and units (the list is KiCad's: Inches, Feet, Millimeters, Centimeter, Mils; off for an SVG), Place at X, Y or interactive placement, Layer (the board's copper then the technical and user layers; unchecked uses the active layer), Group imported items, Fix discontinuities with its Tolerance; the dialog keeps its settings while the page lives | **done** | `dialog_import_graphics.cpp`, `dialog_import_graphics_base.cpp` |
| The DXF reader: LINE, ARC, CIRCLE, POINT (a filled circle), LWPOLYLINE and POLYLINE with bulges (`insertArc`, `MIN_BULGE`), SPLINE (blossoming to cubic pieces; below degree 3 raised, above sampled to lines), ELLIPSE (cubic Beziers, a full one a circle), TEXT and MTEXT (justification, `toNativeString`: formatting codes dropped or turned into KiCad's markup), BLOCK and INSERT (translation, rotation, scale, the arbitrary axis for a mirrored extrusion), lineweights (`370`, by layer or the dialog's), layers, text styles, `$INSUNITS`; DXF's Y axis up becomes the board's down. Entities KiCad does not handle are reported in its words ("DXF dimensions not currently supported.", hatches, solids, images, ...) | **done** (`crates/kicad/src/dxf_import.rs`, 22 unit tests; `tests/dxf_import.rs`, 7, on `tests/fixtures/*.dxf`: a filleted outline rectangle as LINEs and ARCs and as one LWPOLYLINE, holes, bulging slot, nested blocks, millimetres, inches, no unit; KiCad's own example DXFs are read too when `KICAD_SRC_DIR` is set) | `dxf_import_plugin.cpp` (`DXF_IMPORT_PLUGIN`, `DXF_IMPORT_PLUGIN::addLine/addArc/addCircle/addLWPolyline/addSpline/addEllipse/addText/addMText/addInsert`, `insertArc`, `ocsToWcs`, `toNativeString`) |
| Units: the file's `$INSUNITS` wins over the dialog's, a file with none (or unsupported) takes the dialog's, and the dialog's own unit is inches in KiCad's list order but millimetres preselected here (the board's) | **done** (KiCad's `$INSUNITS 0` reset of the caller's unit is kept) | `DXF_IMPORT_PLUGIN::setVariable`, `DXF_IMPORT_UNITS` |
| The SVG reader: `path` (M L H V C S Q T A Z, relative and absolute, arc flags with no separator), `rect` (rounded), `circle`, `ellipse`, `line`, `polyline`, `polygon`, transforms (`matrix`, `translate`, `scale`, `rotate`, `skewX`, `skewY`) and the groups' own, `style` and attribute rules, the 147 named colours, units (`px`, `pt`, `pc`, `mm`, `cm`, `in`, `%`, `em`, `ex` at 96 dpi), `viewBox` scaling; then the plugin's own steps: closed paths as polygons (the stroke's width and dash), open ones as curves, a filled open path as a closed unstroked fill and an open stroke, the nested polygons of one shape combined under its fill rule (Clipper2 union and fracture, holes as slits). `text`, `image` and `use` are reported ("SVG text elements are not currently supported.") | **done** (`crates/kicad/src/svg_import.rs`, 18 unit tests; `tests/svg_import.rs`, 4, on `tests/fixtures/*.svg`: a rounded rect, circles and an ellipse, paths of every kind under transforms, a shape with a hole) | `svg_import_plugin.cpp` (`SVG_IMPORT_PLUGIN::Import`, `DrawPath`, `convertPolygon`, `PostprocessNestedPolygons`), `nanosvg.h` (`nsvg__parse*`, `nsvg__scaleToViewbox`, `nsvg__pathArcTo`) |
| The mapping: `MapCoordinate` (coordinate times the scale plus the offset, in the board's unit), `MapLineWidth` (no width is the default width, the scale applies to it), the size check (a picture over 2147 mm at the scale is refused with "Imported graphic is too large. Maximum scale is ..."; one placed off the range has its offset moved back), `AddArc` (end and middle by rotating the start, a radius over INT_MAX/2 becomes a segment), `setupSplineOrLine` (a curve that is a segment becomes a line, one under 20 nm nothing), a polygon of three points | **done** (`crates/kicad/src/graphics_import.rs` and `crates/ops/src/import_graphics.rs`; 17 verb tests: the layer, the scale and the default width, the origin, the units, the group, a circle, text, the refusals, the JSON form) | `graphics_importer.cpp`, `graphics_importer_buffer.cpp` (`ImportTo`), `graphics_importer_pcbnew.cpp` (`MapCoordinate`, `MapLineWidth`, `AddArc`, `AddPolygon`, `AddSpline`) |
| Place at: the origin typed is divided by the scale (`VECTOR2D origin( m_xOrigin / xscale, ...)`), so a scaled import is placed at origin over scale | **identical** (KiCad's arithmetic kept) | `DIALOG_IMPORT_GRAPHICS::TransferDataFromWindow` |
| Group imported items (two or more items become a group, `Cmd::Group` machinery of section 16) and Fix discontinuities (`ConnectBoardShapes`: the open ends within the tolerance are joined, lines trimmed or extended, an arc's end moved) | **done** (`crates/ops/src/fix_shapes.rs`, 5 tests) | `PCB_CONTROL`/`DRAWING_TOOL::PlaceImportedGraphics` (`ConnectBoardShapes( items, tolerance )`), `pcbnew/convert_shape_list_to_polygon` helpers |
| A board outline from mechanical CAD: a DXF of LINEs and ARCs, or an LWPOLYLINE, imported on Edge.Cuts is the outline kicad-cli finds closed; with a side missing it reports `invalid_outline`; with Fix discontinuities a gap of the tolerance closes | **done**, **slow tests** (`EDA_SLOW_TESTS=1 cargo test -p eda-ops --test import_graphics_kicad_cli`, 3: the written board is read by kicad-cli, its DRC finds a closed outline, its own DXF export reads back as 4 lines and 4 arcs of radius 3, 40 x 30 mm; an SVG polygon outline too) | `BOARD::GetBoardPolygonOutlines`, kicad-cli `pcb drc` |
| "Use as the board outline" (shown when the layer is Edge.Cuts and the board's outline is the intent's polygon): the imported Edge.Cuts become the outline in its place (`DrawingsSection::outline_is_shapes`); refused if they do not close | **studio addition**: that polygon can be neither selected nor deleted, so an outline imported beside it would be read as a cut-out of it | -- |
| Interactive placement (the default): the picture's upper left corner is put at the cursor's grid point, selected, and carried by the Move tool as a Paste is: the command runs with `carry: "begin"` (`crates/cli/src/carry.rs`: the step is held open, nothing is on the undo list yet), a click drops it (the drop joins the step: the import and where it was dropped are **one undo step**), Escape cancels it and leaves nothing behind (no undo and no redo entry). "Place at" puts it where it is told and selects it | **differs**: the shapes are on the board while they are carried (`placeNewItems` in `state/store.tsx`, over `pasteClipboard`'s carry), where KiCad previews them under the cursor and commits at the click | `DRAWING_TOOL::PlaceImportedGraphics` (the preview loop), `PCB_CONTROL::placeBoardItems` |
| Append Board... (`pcbnew.EditorControl.appendBoard`, File menu): a file chooser for a `.kicad_pcb`; the file is read by the server (`POST /api/append_board/read`, `crates/cli/src/append_api.rs`: what it holds, the notes, and the point it is held by, the position of the top left item -- the smallest x, then the smallest y -- by each item's `GetPosition()`: a track's start, an arc's centre, a zone's first corner, a footprint's anchor), then pasted with `at = cursor - reference` through `Cmd::PasteClipboard` (section 5: nets mapped by name, copper on layers the board lacks left out, footprints as board parts, groups kept, a footprint keeps the reference the file gives, `KEEP_ANNOTATIONS`, and is filed under a free one); the step is held open while the Move tool carries the items (a click drops them: the append and its drop are one undo step; Escape leaves nothing behind); an entered group is joined (`Cmd::InGroup`); a footprint keeps its silkscreen, fab drawings and texts (`Footprint::art`, section 28). Strict mode refuses it like any command that makes the board worse | **done** (`kicad-port/appendBoard.ts`, 3 tests; `append_api.rs`, 8 tests: the reference point, the notes, the paste at the cursor putting the top left item there, one undo step, the footprint art kept in the model and in the written file, the refusals) | `PCB_CONTROL::AppendBoard`, `AppendBoardFromFile`, `placeBoardItems` (`aAnchorAtOrigin = false`, `PCB_SELECTION::GetTopLeftItem`) |
| What Append Board does differently: KiCad appends everything (it adds the file's layers to the board and keeps every net). Here copper on a layer this board lacks is left out and an item on a net neither this board nor an appended footprint has is added with no net; a footprint whose reference this board has shows the file's reference, as KiCad's append leaves it ("Don't reannotate dupes on Append Board", `PASTE_MODE::KEEP_ANNOTATIONS`: `pcb_paste_special.rs`), but is filed under the next free one (a part exists once in the intent). A footprint this board already has by its name (or its normalized name) is the board's own definition, with the board's art; the toast after the append says what was left out or changed | **differs**, said to the user (the `notes` of the route) | `PCB_CONTROL::placeBoardItems` |
| File drops: one `components/FileDropHost.tsx` keeps the browser from taking the file and runs the action of the editor's extension table with `{ name, text }` (a browser gives the page the text, not a path): board `.kicad_pcb` and `.brd` Append Board (a `.brd` is refused: "Only KiCad boards (.kicad_pcb files) can be appended."), `.dxf` and `.svg` Import Graphics; Footprint Editor `.kicad_mod` Import Footprint; schematic `.dxf` and `.svg` Import Graphics; the Symbol Editor and the 3D viewer take none. A dashed outline shows while a file is over the window; a dialog open blocks a drop except on Import Graphics, which takes a graphic into its File: field | **done** (`kicad-port/fileDrops.ts`, 4 tests; `kicad-port/actionTabGate.ts` keeps each action to its tab) | `EDA_BASE_FRAME::OnDropFiles`, `pcb_edit_frame.cpp` / `footprint_edit_frame.cpp` / `sch_edit_frame.cpp` (`m_acceptedExts`), `PCB_CONTROL::DdAppendBoard` |
| A drop of several files: KiCad runs each one's action in turn; here the first the studio can open is opened and the rest are said to be skipped (an open dialog or a carried placement would meet the next); a file whose extension the frame does not take is ignored by KiCad and said so here | **differs** | `EDA_BASE_FRAME::DoWithAcceptedFiles` |
| Import Netlist (`pcbnew.EditorControl.importNetlist`) | **not ported** (package P8c, low priority: the parts and nets come from the intent) | `board_editor_control.cpp::ImportNetlist` |

Rust (this section): `crates/kicad` 8 + 22 + 18 unit and 11 integration tests (`graphics_import.rs`, `dxf_import.rs`, `svg_import.rs`, `tests/{dxf,svg}_import.rs`), `crates/ops` 5 + 17 (`fix_shapes.rs`, `import_graphics_tests.rs`) and `crates/ops/tests/import_graphics_kicad_cli.rs` (3, slow),
`crates/cli` 3 + 8 + 3 + 1 (`graphics_api.rs`, `append_api.rs`, the undo steps in `board.rs` -- the board's import, the schematic's and the Symbol Editor's, and a schematic import carried in a held-open step --, a dropped `.kicad_mod`'s art in `library_api.rs`). Web: `kicad-port/{importGraphics,appendBoard,fileDrops}.test.ts` (16 + 3 + 4) and 3 new lines in `actionTabGate.test.ts`.

Verified in the browser (port 8819, a scratch board made with `eda board new examples/mcu_board_30plus.yaml`; the page's pointer events and the drops were dispatched in the page, the real pointer never touched): `e2e/files-in.check.js` runs 102 checks through `window.__eda` --
the actions and the File menu's entries are wired; a DXF dropped on the board opens the dialog with the file and the server's description ("8 items: 4 lines, 4 arcs"); OK with Place at puts the four lines and four arcs on Edge.Cuts at the origin (x 0 to 40 mm, y -30 to 0), 0.2 mm wide, grouped and selected as one undo step, and the outline replaces the intent's polygon;
the scale doubles the picture and the default width, the origin is divided by the scale; interactive placement puts the corner at the cursor, the Move tool carries it, Escape takes it back; a DXF with no unit takes the dialog's, one that names it keeps it; Fix discontinuities joins corners 0.3 mm apart; an SVG becomes polygons of the stroke's own width; a file with nothing to import,
a scale of zero, a file no plugin handles and a file that is not a DXF are refused in KiCad's words; Append Board's drop is refused under strict mode when the part would land outside the outline, and with strict off pastes the file's part, track, line and text with the line's start at the cursor and the part 15, 15 mm on, the Move tool carrying them, Escape undoing the one step, a click dropping them at a second place;
then the schematic and the Symbol Editor (PARITY-sch.md section 20, PARITY-symedit.md) and the drops on every tab, a DataTransfer with a File dispatched to the window. A hidden Browser pane never fits the board to its canvas (no animation frames), so the script fits it itself with Zoom to Fit before it moves the pointer.

**Approximations, and what is left.**
- The DXF's text is the board's own stroke text: its size is the file's height (the file's character width is not kept), its vertical justification is a shift of the anchor by half a height, a text inside a turned block is sized and turned with it (KiCad's matrix sets it on its side).
- Nested DXF blocks are expanded properly (a block that inserts a block, with a guard for a block that inserts itself and a depth of 16): KiCad's plugin drops the inner insert. A spline of degree above 3 is drawn as line segments, a full circle or ellipse arc is a circle (KiCad makes a degenerate arc), the second unit scaling KiCad applies to an ellipse of ratio 1 is not applied.
- An SVG gradient is a filled shape, a pattern is not drawn; the `use` element, `image` and `text` are reported and not imported.
- Interactive placement carries the shapes already on the board (above) where KiCad previews them; a footprint dropped from a `.kicad_mod` on the board is not accepted (the board editor's frame does not take one).
- Import Netlist, the schematic's Import Sheet by a drop (it reads a file by its path on the server; a dropped file has none), the schematic's image drop (the IR has no bitmap item) and the Footprint Editor's Import Graphics (`placeImportedGraphics` on the Footprint tab; the board's dialog does not open there) are left.

## 30. Footprint assignment, exchange, compare and reannotate (GAPS.md item 26, package P2; 2026-10-12)

Before this section a part's footprint came from the intent and nothing on the board could change it: the schematic symbol's Footprint field (Symbol Properties, Assign Footprints, the Symbol Fields Table) changed the schematic and nothing else, Change Footprint did not
exist, Update Footprints from Library never read a library, and a reference rename (Edit Reference, Annotate with reset, Increment Annotations) left the old reference's footprint, pose, routing, fields, edits, lock and group where they were while the next reconcile made a fresh
unplaced part. Now each is a verb of its own: a rename carries the part, the footprint a part is on is a per-instance assignment the schematic's field sets, and the tools above work on it. Ported from `pcbnew/pcb_edit_frame.cpp` (`ExchangeFootprint`), `dialogs/dialog_exchange_footprints.cpp`,
`netlist_reader/board_netlist_updater.cpp` (`replaceFootprint`, `updateFootprintParameters`), `tools/global_edit_tool.cpp` (`ExchangeFootprints`), `drc/drc_test_provider_library_parity.cpp` (`FOOTPRINT::FootprintNeedsUpdate`), `tools/board_inspection_tool.cpp` (`DiffFootprint`),
`common/widgets/footprint_diff_widget.cpp`, `tools/board_reannotate_tool.cpp`, `dialogs/dialog_board_reannotate.cpp`, `footprint_libraries_utils.cpp` (`SaveFootprintToBoard`) and `tools/pcb_control.cpp` (`SaveFpToBoard`) at `8303b2ad`. The IR change is additive (`DrawingsSection::renamed_parts` and
`footprint_assignments`, serde defaults: a design that has none is byte for byte what it was) and kicad-cli is still the only engine that judges the board.

| Behavior | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| A rename carries the board's part (`Board::rename_part`, `crates/ops/src/part_rename.rs`): the pose and the floorplan block, the pads the routing ends on, the net list's pins, the footprint's edits (fields, attributes, pad edits), its assignment, the imported mask facts, the zone overrides, a footprint made on the board, the lock, the groups and the DRC waivers that name its pads, for every `old -> new` at once (two parts can swap). The intent's part itself is recorded in `drawings.renamed_parts`, which `board::load` lays over the intent, so the model has the part, its footprint and its nets under the new reference. `rename_symbol` (Edit Reference, `U`), `annotate` (Annotate with reset) and `increment_annotations` all end there; a new reference that is another footprint of the board with no symbol is refused | **done** | `BOARD_NETLIST_UPDATER::updateFootprintParameters` ("Changed %s reference designator to %s."): KiCad sets the footprint's reference from the netlist's; the studio's schematic and board are one design, so the rename moves both |
| One undo step on both editors: a rename, a Change Footprint, an Update from Library, a reannotation and a symbol's Footprint field that moves the board are steps of the whole design (`Cmd::spans_both_editors`, the history's `both` tag), so one Ctrl+Z from either tab takes the schematic's half and the board's back (a Pcb-tab undo skips a pure schematic step and the other way round, as before) | **done** | (KiCad's two documents each have their own undo) |
| The footprint a part is on is a per-instance assignment (`DrawingsSection::footprint_assignments`: reference, footprint name, and the footprint's own copy when the model does not resolve that name), laid over the model by `board::build_model` (`eda_model::fp_assign`) beside `footprint_edits`; an undo step restores it with the rest | **done** | `FOOTPRINT::SetFPID` / `BOARD_NETLIST_UPDATER::replaceFootprint` |
| The schematic symbol's Footprint field is the board's footprint (`Cmd::EditSymbolFields`, `SetSymbolFields`, Assign Footprints, Import Footprint Assignments, Symbol Properties): the server adds the exchange of the part's footprint to the command (`crates/cli/src/library_place.rs`, the library copy from the tables, the Footprint Editor's entry or what the model knows), with every option on and pad positions matched, as `replaceFootprint` calls `ExchangeFootprint`; a locked footprint is not changed ("Could not change %s footprint from '%s' to '%s' (footprint is locked)") and a footprint no library has is left (the schematic keeps what was typed) -- F8 lists both | **done** | `board_netlist_updater.cpp::replaceFootprint` |
| Update PCB from Schematic (F8) lists the footprints a symbol names that the board has not taken ("Change R1 footprint from 'A' to 'B'.", "Cannot change ... (footprint is locked).", "Cannot update ... (footprint ... not found).") and applies the ones it can as one batch; Update Schematic from PCB sets each such symbol's Footprint field to the board's (the back-annotation) | **done** | `DIALOG_UPDATE_PCB`, `BOARD_NETLIST_UPDATER`, `BACK_ANNOTATE` |
| Change Footprint / Change Footprints (`pcbnew.GlobalEdit.changeFootprint`, `changeFootprints`; the dialog's own buttons in Footprint Properties): this footprint, the footprints with the same reference pattern, the same value (`WildCompareString`), the same footprint id, the selected ones, put on a library id with the dialog's options (match pad positions, remove text items if not in the library footprint, update or reset text layers, text sizes and styles, text position and rotation, text content, fabrication attributes, clearance overrides, 3D models). `Cmd::ExchangeFootprints`, one undo step; the report is the message window (`processFootprint`: "Changed footprint R1 from 'A' to 'B': OK", "*** library footprint not found ***") | **done** | `DIALOG_EXCHANGE_FOOTPRINTS`, `GLOBAL_EDIT_TOOL::ExchangeFootprints`, `PCB_EDIT_FRAME::ExchangeFootprint` |
| The exchange keeps the pose's side, label side, lock and group; with "Match pad positions" the position and orientation move so the pads whose number is unique land where the old ones were (`eda_model::fp_exchange::pad_alignment`: the best-matching translation when more than half the pads agree, else the mean, the 90 degree snap, a bottom-side footprint in its mirrored frame); the pads are the new footprint's, on their nets by pad number, the pad edits dropped; the Reference and Value layouts, the library's texts and the user fields follow the text options (texts matched by similarity, `Levenshtein`); a text with no match stays where it was unless removal is on; attributes, clearance overrides and 3D models follow their options | **done**, pad nets by number only | `ComputeFootprintShift`, `ORTHO_ITEM_REALIGNER`, `processTextItem`, `ExchangeFootprint` |
| Update Footprint / Update Footprints from Library (`updateFootprint`, `updateFootprints`): each footprint is put on the library copy of the footprint it is on, read through the library tables (`LibSource`, the Footprint Editor's entry first where the project edited one); the plan (`POST /api/exchange_plan`) says which footprints have a library copy and which differ from it (`FOOTPRINT::FootprintNeedsUpdate`, `eda_model::fp_compare`: the type, attributes, overrides, graphics in their order and pads paired by number, with KiCad's messages), "(no changes)" is told and nothing is changed for one that does not, and the message window lists under an updated footprint what the library copy has otherwise | **done** | `DIALOG_EXCHANGE_FOOTPRINTS` (update mode), `FOOTPRINT::FootprintNeedsUpdate` |
| Compare Footprint with Library (`pcbnew.InspectionTool.DiffFootprint`): one footprint (the selection or the one under the cursor; else "Select a footprint to diff with its library equivalent."); the dialog's Summary page ("Board vs library diff for:", the footprint, library and item, what differs or "No relevant differences detected.", "The library is not included in the current configuration.", "The library no longer contains the item %s."), the Visual page (both footprints drawn over each other in the footprint's frame, the slider from Board to Library with KiCad's fade, Swap and the `/` key) and "Update Footprint from Library..." that selects the footprint and opens the update dialog on it (`GET /api/footprint_diff`, `components/FootprintDiffDialog.tsx`) | **done** | `BOARD_INSPECTION_TOOL::DiffFootprint`, `FOOTPRINT_DIFF_WIDGET` |
| Geographical Reannotate (`pcbnew.ReannotateTool.ShowReannotateDialog`): the dialog's eight orders (top to bottom or left to right, each way), the location (the footprint or its Reference text) rounded to a grid, the scope (all, front, back, selection), exclude locked footprints, the exclusion list (prefixes, or patterns ending in `*`), the front and back start numbers (the back's blank continues the front's) and prefixes (added once, or removed), the back sorted as a mirror image; the message window lists the front and back footprints in their order with their positions, the prefixes in use, the change log, the duplicates, and warns of references with no number before it asks "Reannotate anyway?". `Cmd::ReannotateBoard` renames every symbol on every sheet and every board part at once, so a swap of two numbers leaves nothing under an old reference; one undo step on both editors (KiCad leaves the schematic to Update Schematic from PCB) | **done**, the schematic follows | `DIALOG_BOARD_REANNOTATE::BuildFootprintList`, `BuildUnavailableRefsList`, `BuildChangeArray`, `ReannotateBoard`; `UTIL::GetRefDesNumber`; `StrNumCmp` |
| Insert footprint into PCB (`pcbnew.ModuleEditor.saveFootprintToBoard`) from the Footprint Editor: where parts name the footprint it updates them (as before: `Cmd::UpdateFootprintOnBoard`); where none does it inserts the Footprint Editor's copy as a part of its own (`Cmd::PlaceFootprint` with the editor's definition, the next free reference of the footprint's usual prefix, its name as the value, at the centre of the board, the project's entry published in the same step), one undo step, the board shown with it selected. KiCad hangs the new footprint on the board editor's cursor; here it is placed and selected | **done**, placed rather than carried | `FOOTPRINT_EDIT_FRAME::SaveFootprintToBoard` (the insert branch) |
| Swap Pad Nets, Swap Gate Nets | **not ported** (not reached; the way in is in item 26 of `GAPS.md`: a verb that rotates the nets of the selected pads in `design.nets`, with the tracks that end on them following, which makes the board disagree with the schematic as in KiCad; Swap Gate Nets also needs the footprint's unit info) | `EDIT_TOOL::SwapPadNets` |

Rust (76 new tests): `crates/model/src/fp_assign.rs` (4: an id's reference, the model with the part under its new reference everywhere and a swap, a rename of a part the model lacks, renames composing through a second one and vanishing on the way back), `fp_exchange.rs` (9: the pad alignment with another origin and turned a quarter, a pad that shares its number is no point to go by, the best matches or the mean, the 90 degree snap, `Levenshtein`, the text matching and the texts the options leave alone or nothing matches), `fp_compare.rs` (8: KiCad's order and words, pads paired by number, a stroke on copper only, the courtyard, a polygon started at another vertex), `reannotate.rs` (16: `RoundToGrid` with C's remainders, `StrNumCmp`, the eight directions on a block of four for each side, the grid rounding, the start numbers and the back's continuation, the unavailable numbers, the exclusion list and the scope's last word, prefixes, a reference with no number, the change log's order, duplicates and the abort);
`crates/ops/src/part_rename_tests.rs` (6: the rename moves everything and leaves nothing, the second rename moves its entry on, a board-made footprint is not taken, Increment Annotations in one go, Annotate with reset swaps two parts), `fp_exchange_tests.rs` (9: Change Footprint keeps the pose, nets, lock and group, each option, remove text items, match pad positions, a footprint no library has refuses the whole command, Update from Library, a footprint made on the board, the pending changes of a symbol's field, a rename after an exchange), `reannotate_tests.rs` (8: the three-way places, the directions, a back footprint's own start, a part that is not on the board keeps its number, the selection, a board-only footprint, the plan changes nothing, both editors);
`crates/cli/src/fp_assign_tests.rs` (14: a rename through the board directory and its undo from either tab, Annotate with reset, the symbol's field followed and undone, F8 and a locked footprint, Change Footprint of several parts, the exchange plan, a reannotation with the schematic following, the command read from the studio's JSON, the selection scope, Insert footprint into PCB; **slow tier**: kicad-cli's `--schematic-parity` after a rename, after a Change Footprint and after a reannotation says what it said before, and the derived `.kicad_pcb` and `.kicad_sch` name the new references) and `fp_library_tests.rs` (2: an update reads the library copy and lists what differs, Compare Footprint with Library).
Web (28 new `node --test` cases): `kicad-port/updatePcb.ts` and `pcbExchange.ts` (what F8 lists, what the message window says), `footprintDiff.ts` (the summary, the slider's opacities, the bounds, the arc through three points, the pads), `reannotate.ts` (the form, the start numbers, the prefix filter, KiCad's window, the warning), `fpInsert.ts`.

Verified in the browser (port 8824, a scratch board made with `eda board new examples/mcu_board_30plus.yaml` and its parts placed; the real pointer never touched): `e2e/fp-assign.check.js` runs 8 scenarios (69 checks) through `window.__eda` -- a rename carries the part (pose, footprint, pads, no stranded route, the symbol under the new reference, one Ctrl+Z); the Footprint field is followed, F8 lists a locked footprint and applies it once unlocked;
Change Footprint on one footprint and on the footprints of one value; Update from Library lists a pad that differs and a second update says "(no changes)"; Compare Footprint with Library (the summary, the slider's opacities, Swap, the update it opens, a built-in package's message); Geographical Reannotate (the window, the numbers by place, every footprint with its symbol, one undo); Insert footprint into PCB from the Footprint Editor; and the board, the schematic's symbols and the project footprint library as they were, with no console error.

**Approximations, and what is left.**
- Swap Pad Nets and Swap Gate Nets (above, not reached); Import Netlist (a part from a netlist has no schematic symbol and the schematic is the master).
- Pad nets by number only: KiCad's `ExchangeFootprint` also matches the pads that share no number by their position and name; here a pad's net is a fact of its pin, so a pad the new footprint has under no known number is on no net.
- `FootprintNeedsUpdate` compares what the model keeps of a library footprint (pads, graphics, courtyard, attributes derived from the pads, the overrides); the file's attribute flags, net-tie groups, rule areas and a footprint's own stackup layers are not kept, so those lines (and the stackup remarks) are not reported. The drawings of Compare Footprint with Library are the footprint in its own frame; the Visual page draws pads as rectangles, pills and ellipses (a trapezoid, a chamfered or a custom pad as its box) with their holes, and texts are not drawn.
- A footprint whose library is not in the tables is "not found" for an update, as KiCad says; a built-in package (`0603`, `SOIC-8`) has no library, so Update and Compare tell so; the Footprint Editor's entry of that name is the copy they use when the project has one.
- The reannotation follows the quirks of KiCad's source (the scope has the last word over the exclusion list; a reference with no number is numbered by its whole name in a scope other than "All"), and it takes the numbers of the parts that are not on the board (a symbol not placed yet) as taken. Ties in a place are numbered in the order of the old references (KiCad's `std::sort` leaves them unspecified).
- Insert footprint into PCB places the footprint at the centre of the board (the Strict switch may refuse it where the centre is occupied) instead of carrying it on the cursor; the Footprint Viewer's `AddFootprintToPCB` is not a tab here.

## 31. The 3D models of a footprint: the 3D Models tab, the model list's verbs and Migrate 3D Models (GAPS.md item 30, package P14; 2026-10-13)

Before this section a footprint's `(model ...)` entries (`Footprint::models3d`: file name, offset, scale, rotation, opacity, hide) were read from the libraries and from boards, written back and drawn by the 3D viewer (`89ff669`), and nothing edited them: the board's Footprint Properties dialog had no 3D Models tab,
`FootprintEdit` had no model list, the Footprint Editor's dialog had one text field for one path, the Footprint Chooser had no 3D view and Migrate 3D Models was "needs a place to write the substitution" (`STALE-REASONS.md` SR3). Now a footprint's list is an edit of its own with its verbs, the tab is KiCad's in both dialogs
(`PARITY-fpedit.md` section 12 has the Footprint Editor's half), the file it ends in is KiCad's and Migrate 3D Models works on the board. Ported from `pcbnew/dialogs/panel_fp_properties_3d_model.cpp`, `dialog_footprint_properties.cpp`, `3d-viewer/dialogs/panel_preview_3d_model.cpp`, `dialog_select_3d_model.cpp`, `pcbnew/dialogs/dialog_migrate_3d_models.cpp`,
`3d-viewer/3d_cache/model_substitution_helpers.cpp`, `common/filename_resolver.cpp` (`ValidateFileName`, `ShortenPath`), `pcb_io_kicad_sexpr.cpp` (`format( FOOTPRINT )`, `FormatDouble2Str`) and `pcbnew/footprint_chooser_frame.cpp` at `8303b2ad`. The IR change is additive (`FootprintEdit::models3d`, `ConstraintModel::edited_models`, serde
defaults: a design that has none is byte for byte what it was) and kicad-cli is still the only engine that reads the file.

| Behavior | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| A footprint on the board keeps its list of 3D models as an edit of its own: `FootprintEdit::models3d` is the whole list (`None` is the list the footprint has by itself, from its library or from the board file it was read from; `Some` is what was set on the board, an empty list included), laid over the footprint by `eda_model::fp_edit::edited_footprint` in `board::fold_footprint_edits`, so the 3D view, the `.kicad_pcb` writer and every export see it. A list that comes to what the footprint has by itself is not kept as an edit, so an update from the library still brings its models; a list set to nothing stays empty (`ConstraintModel::edited_models`: `eda_kicad::part_models` would otherwise give an emptied footprint the package's generic KiCad model) | **done** | `FOOTPRINT::Models()`, `DIALOG_FOOTPRINT_PROPERTIES::TransferDataFromWindow` |
| The verbs, each one undo step and each refusing what would change nothing or cannot be written (so no empty step): `set_board_models` (the whole list: what the dialog's OK and Migrate send), `add_board_model` (at the end, or before a position), `remove_board_model`, `move_board_model` (the order of the rows), `edit_board_model` (file name, offset, scale, rotation, opacity, show: only the fields given), and `models3d` on `edit_footprint_properties` (the list with the rest of a dialog's edit, one step). A bad position says "there is no model 3: the footprint has 2 3D models" (`ops_bad_model_index`), a file name that cannot be used "No filename entered" or "Illegal filename" with the name (`ops_bad_model`), a list or a field that is as it was `ops_nothing_to_change`; a model the numbers of which the panel would not take (an offset past 1000 mm, a scale outside 0.0001 to 10000, an opacity outside 0 to 1) is refused with the number and its range | **done** | `PANEL_FP_PROPERTIES_3D_MODEL::OnAdd3DRow`, `OnRemove3DModel`, `OnAdd3DModel`; `PANEL_PREVIEW_3D_MODEL` (`MAX_SCALE`, `MAX_OFFSET`) |
| File names are checked as `FILENAME_RESOLVER::ValidateFileName` checks them (the macOS and Linux branch: `*` and `?` are the forbidden characters; an alias may not hold any of `{ } [ ] ( ) % ~ < > " = ' ; : . , & ? / $` or a backtick, a backslash or a vertical bar; a name may not end in a colon; a `file://` or `kicad-embed://` name must name something that is not a path from the root) and lose the line breaks and tabs a paste brings (`cleanupFilename`); an empty name is "No filename entered". That the file is there is no condition: the tab shows a missing file with its icon and keeps the row, as KiCad does | **done** | `FILENAME_RESOLVER::ValidateFileName`, `PANEL_FP_PROPERTIES_3D_MODEL::cleanupFilename`, `updateValidateStatus` |
| The 3D Models tab in Footprint Properties (the board's dialog is `General` and `3D Models` tabs): the grid (a status cell, the file name, Show), Add (a blank row, `OnAdd3DRow`), Browse (the model chooser), Remove, Configure Paths..., and beside it what the preview panel has for the selected row: Scale, Rotation and Offset fields with their spin buttons (steps 0.1 and 0.02 with Shift, 90 and 1 degrees, 0.5 and 0.1 mm or 25 and 5 mil by the editor's units) and the wheel, the Opacity slider, the preview with the six views, the orthographic and board-body switches and Reload. The fields take arithmetic (`evaluateTextCtrl`: `2*1.27`, `-(3+1)/2`), the rotation is shown the other way round ("positive-is-CW" in the file, counter-clockwise in the dialog) and `rotationFromString`'s clamp is kept (a value past 180 is cut to its first digits); nothing is sent until OK, which sends one `set_board_models` or nothing when the list is the one the footprint has. A row with no file name is not sent (KiCad keeps it in the grid and does not write it). The status cell is the server's (`POST /api/3dmodel/check`: "No filename entered", "Illegal filename", "File not found", and the studio's own `forbidden` with its reason for a file it will not serve: outside KiCad's 3D model directory, the board's folder and the folders its path variables name, or not a `.wrl`, `.step` or `.stp`) | **done**; the "Extruded 3D Body" box and the embedded files are not | `PANEL_FP_PROPERTIES_3D_MODEL`, `PANEL_PREVIEW_3D_MODEL::onXRotationChange` etc., `evaluateTextCtrl`, `rotationFromString` |
| The preview draws the footprint as the preview panel does ("shown like in Fp editor: rot 0, not flipped": its pads, drawings and texts in its own frame on a piece of board) with the models of the list that are shown, placed as KiCad places them (`get3dModelsFromFootprint`, `kicad-port/model3d.ts`), by the 3D view's own board builder and model cache (`viewer3d/modelCache.ts`: a STEP is converted once by kicad-cli, a VRML read as it is), the camera the ported trackball; the footprint's geometry is the server's (`GET /api/3dmodel/preview?part=R1` or `?footprint=Lib:Name`, the pads from `placed_pads`) | **done** | `PANEL_PREVIEW_3D_MODEL::UpdateDummyFootprint`, `RENDER_3D_OPENGL::get3dModelsFromFootprint` |
| The model chooser (Browse; `DIALOG_SELECT_3DMODEL`): a list of folders to walk -- the board's own, the ones its path variables name, KiCad's 3D model library -- with the model files of each (STEP and VRML; the filter All, STEP, VRML), a viewer for the file selected and Configure Paths...; the answer is the path a footprint writes, the file shortened to the first folder that holds it (`ShortenPath`: `${KICAD10_3DMODEL_DIR}/Resistor_SMD.3dshapes/R_0603_1608Metric.step`, `${KIPRJMOD}/parts/chip.step`); a chosen model starts with KiCad's defaults. The server lists only what `GET /api/3dmodel` may read (`GET /api/3dmodel/browse`), so the page never holds a path of the machine it cannot use | **done**; it lists the folders the studio may read, not the whole disk | `DIALOG_SELECT_3DMODEL`, `FILENAME_RESOLVER::ShortenPath` |
| The `.kicad_pcb` writer and reader: each footprint's models as `PCB_IO_KICAD_SEXPR::format( FOOTPRINT )` writes them (the file name, `(hide yes)`, `(opacity 0.6500)` with four decimals when it is not 1, `(offset (xyz ..))`, `(scale (xyz ..))`, `(rotate (xyz ..))` with `FormatDouble2Str`'s ten significant digits), read back by `parseFOOTPRINT`; a model with no file name is not written; a bottom-side footprint's placement is turned by a half turn about z on the way out and in (`Model3d::flipped_frame`: the studio keeps the list in the top-side frame, where KiCad stores a flipped footprint mirrored in y and turns its model with it) so the model stands on the pads in KiCad's render. kicad-cli's `pcb upgrade` writes our board out again with the same entries character for character, and `pcb export vrml` of ours and of its copy is the same (slow-tier proof, below) | **done** | `PCB_IO_KICAD_SEXPR::format( FOOTPRINT )` (the `(model` block), `FormatDouble2Str`, `parseMODEL`, `RENDER_3D_OPENGL::get3dModelsFromFootprint` |
| A footprint from the Footprint Editor or from the project library brings its models to the board with it (Insert footprint into PCB, Place Footprint, Update Footprint on Board, Update Footprint from Library); Change Footprint(s)' "Update/reset 3D models" option (on by default) takes the new library footprint's models, and off the footprint keeps the list it had -- one the board set stays set, an emptied one too (`exchange_one`) | **done** | `FOOTPRINT_EDIT_FRAME::SaveFootprintToBoard`, `PCB_EDIT_FRAME::ExchangeFootprint` (`aResetModels`) |
| The 3D viewer draws what the list says (offset, scale, rotation, opacity, hidden models not drawn), and so do the STEP and VRML exports (`kicad-cli pcb export step`, `vrml`): the model of a footprint on the board is `eda_kicad::part_models`, which takes the list set on the board first | **done** | `RENDER_3D_OPENGL::get3dModelsFromFootprint`, `EXPORTER_STEP`, `EXPORTER_VRML` |
| The Footprint Chooser's 3D view (the chooser of Place Footprint, Change Footprint and the Footprint Editor): the footprint view and the 3D view are two toggles, both can be on, the last one on cannot be turned off, the footprint view alone is how it opens and the choice is kept; the 3D view draws the library footprint selected with its models (`GET /api/3dmodel/preview?footprint=`) | **done**; "Show 3D viewer in own window" is not | `FOOTPRINT_CHOOSER_FRAME` (`m_grButton3DView`, `m_show3DMode`, `m_preview3DCanvas`, `m_show3DViewer`) |
| Migrate 3D Models (`pcbnew.GlobalEdit.migrate3DModels`, Tools menu): the dialog always opens. Left: the `.wrl` and `.wrz` models the board's footprints name that no search finds (bold while the row has no replacement chosen; the tooltip names the footprints); middle: the STEP-family files of the folders the resolver searches (the board's, its path variables', KiCad's library, the board's `3dshapes`) that could stand for it, ranked as `rankCandidatesFor` ranks them (a file of the same normalised name 1000 points when it sits in a folder of the reference's name, 800 when not, a few edits away 500 less ten each, the first fifteen, then "(keep existing reference)") with the best chosen for every row; Add Search Directory... (a folder inside what the studio may read is scanned too and every row ranked again, a row keeping its pick when that file is still there, else the new best), Open External File... (a file picked in the model chooser, STEP only, becomes the row's first candidate "user-selected" with 2000 points, or is chosen where the row has it); right: the preview of the first footprint with the candidate in the placement the missing model had.
