Replace Models sends one `migrate_board_models` (the old name for the new in every footprint that has it, the placement kept, one undo step); with nothing chosen it is Keep, as is Keep Existing and Escape. The folders added are kept in the browser between sessions | **done**; the folders are those the studio may read, the load-time migration is not | `DIALOG_MIGRATE_3D_MODELS` (`collectMissingModels`, `buildCatalog`, `rankCandidatesFor`, `OnAddSearchDirectoryClick`, `OnOpenExternalFileClick`, `OnReplaceClick`), `MODEL_SUBSTITUTION`, `GLOBAL_EDIT_TOOL::Migrate3DModels` |

Rust (52 new tests, one slow-tier): `crates/model/src/fp_models.rs` (10: `ValidateFileName`'s rules one case each, a pasted name, add / remove / move / edit by position, a position the list has none at refused in words, the numbers the panel takes, an edit that changes nothing refused) and `model_subst.rs` (6: the extensions, the normalised stem, the points of a candidate by folder, the edit distance, the first fifteen);
`crates/kicad/src/footprint_lib.rs` (2: the models written as KiCad writes them and read back with their opacity and hide, a library footprint exports its whole list), `lib.rs` (1: `FormatDouble2Str`'s ten significant digits and sixteen decimals for a small number), `model3d.rs` (1: an emptied list is empty, not the package's);
`crates/ops/src/fp_models_tests.rs` (16: add, edit, move and remove each one command, a model added before a position, the whole list in one command and an empty list a footprint with no model, the dialog's fields keeping the pad and field edits, a position the list has none at refused with its reason, a file name or a number the panel would not take refused in its words, a command that changes nothing refused so that no empty undo step is pushed, a batch that a refusal undoes whole, the list in and out of the design file, the commands read from the studio's JSON, a Change Footprint keeping the list unless the models are reset; Migrate: the new name in every footprint that has the old with its placement, only the names given, a bad new name refused, the command read from JSON) and `tests.rs` (1: `edit_footprint_properties` takes the whole list);
`crates/cli/src/fp_models_tests.rs` (8: a list set on a footprint reaches the 3D view's model, the board file and one undo takes it back; a refused command is no step; a footprint with every model taken off has none; the models follow their footprint and a bottom one is written in KiCad's frame; a list set in the Footprint Editor reaches the board and a board's own list wins; a footprint placed from the project library brings its models; Migrate 3D Models through a board directory; **slow tier**: kicad-cli reads the models this studio writes as it reads its own), `model3d_tab_api.rs` (7: the status cell's words, the chooser's roots and a folder's paths, the chooser not leaving what the model route may read, a path shortened to the first root that holds it, the catalog's STEP files once each, a name found by a file of that name and a `.wrl` not by its STEP, the preview's footprint).
Web (20 new `node --test` cases): `kicad-port/fpModels.ts` (14: a part's models as rows, what OK sends and nothing for the list the footprint has, arithmetic in a field, a half-typed number, the fields' decimals, the rotation's sign and its clamp that keeps 200 as 20, an offset in the editor's units, the spin buttons' and the wheel's steps, the opacity, the status, a row moved, the library's list and the Footprint Editor's OK), `kicad-port/migrate3d.ts` (6: the choice a row starts on, what Replace sends and nothing for Keep, a file picked by hand, the choices kept when a folder is added, the preview's placement, the folders kept once).

Verified in the browser (port 8826, a scratch board made with `eda board new examples/mcu_board_30plus.yaml` and its parts placed; the real pointer never touched): `e2e/fp-models.check.js` runs 11 scenarios (153 checks) through `window.__eda` -- the tab lists the models with the numbers as KiCad shows them and the preview draws them; the fields (an expression, a unit, the steps, the rotation's sign, Show); Add, Remove and
the order of rows; Browse walks KiCad's library, previews a file and adds it with the defaults; OK is one step and one undo takes it back (the 3D view, the board file); the verbs' refusals; the Footprint Chooser's 3D view; the Footprint Editor's dialog with the tab; Migrate 3D Models (empty lists with nothing missing, the `.wrl` of two footprints, the best candidate chosen, "keep existing"
bolding the row, a file picked by hand, a folder added, Escape closing the chooser first, Replace in one step and one undo); and the board as it was, with no console error. Slow tier (`EDA_SLOW_TESTS=1`): a board whose footprint has two models -- offsets, scales, rotations, an opacity, one hidden -- is written by us, read and written again by kicad-cli (`pcb upgrade`) with the same entries, and both boards export the same VRML.

**Approximations, and what is left.**
- The "Extruded 3D Body" box of the tab (`EXTRUDED_3D_BODY`: a body made from the courtyard, fab or silkscreen outline or the pins' box, its height, standoff, material and colour, its export to STEP) and the embedded files (`PANEL_EMBEDDED_FILES`, `kicad-embed://` models, Collect and Embed 3D Models, which needs the zstd of GAPS.md item 24) are not ported; a `kicad-embed://` name is legal and kept, and not resolved.
- For a footprint on the bottom side the tab shows the numbers in the footprint's top-side frame (the one the list is kept in; the preview is "rot 0, not flipped"), where KiCad shows the file's numbers, which a half turn about z apart; the file this writes is KiCad's, so a board that goes to KiCad and comes back reads the same.
- Migrate 3D Models: the folders it searches are the ones the studio may read (KiCad's library, the board's folder, the folders its path variables name; Configure Paths... adds one), where KiCad's folder picker is the operating system's; the candidates come from STEP-family files, and the chooser the studio shows for Open External File... lists STEP and VRML (an IGES file is a candidate when it is in a searched folder, but is not drawn). The load-time part of KiCad's migration (`AutoMigrateByFilename` renaming a reference with one exact sibling, and the info bar "N WRL 3D models could not be matched" with its "Show options" link) is not done: a board opens with the references it has.
- The model chooser lists the model files of the folders it may read, not the whole disk (a browser is given nothing else); a file outside them can be typed in, and the status cell says why the studio will not serve it.
- "Show 3D viewer in own window" of the Footprint Chooser is not a thing here (the 3D view is a pane of the chooser).
- A built-in package (`0603`, `SOIC-8`) opened in the Footprint Editor has no model list there (its generic model is added only when a footprint is placed from the libraries); the board's tab shows the generic model the package has.

## 32. Search and inspection (GAPS.md items 21 and 29, package P11; 2026-10-12)

Ported from `pcbnew/dialogs/dialog_find_by_properties.cpp`, `pcbnew/pcbexpr_evaluator.cpp`, `pcbexpr_functions.cpp`, `common/libeval_compiler/{libeval_compiler.cpp,grammar.lemon}`, `common/properties/property_mgr.cpp` (32a), `pcbnew/tools/board_inspection_tool.cpp`, `pcbnew/drc/{drc_engine,drc_rule_parser,drc_rule_condition,drc_rule}.cpp` and `common/dsnlexer.cpp` (32c) at `8303b2ad`; the Footprint Checker is `PARITY-fpedit.md` section 12. Nothing here is a DRC: kicad-cli stays the only engine that checks the board; the inspections show which rule wins where.

### 32a. Find by Properties

| Behavior | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| Edit > Find by Properties... (`pcbnew.EditorControl.findByProperties`) opens the dialog: a floating, modeless pane that is created once and only hidden when closed (the board stays in reach, and what it selects is in view); its rows are built when it is shown, when the tab goes back to From Selection and each time the selection changes | **done** (`components/FindByPropertiesDialog.tsx`, `state/findByProperties.ts`, `actions/findByPropertiesActions.ts`) | `PCB_EDIT_FRAME::ShowFindByPropertiesDialog`, `NotifyFindByPropertiesDialog`, `DIALOG_FIND_BY_PROPERTIES::Show`, `OnSelectionChanged`, `onNotebookPageChanged` |
| From Selection: the label says what is selected ("No items selected", the friendly name of the one item, "N objects selected"); the grid has Property, Value and Match. The rows are the properties every selected class has by name that are visible (hidden from the design editors, or from the Properties panel except Type, are not offered), available for each item and with a value, in the alphabetical order of their names (not the panel's order); the value is what the Properties panel shows (a length in the display unit, a choice by its label) and `<...>` when the items differ, and a Match of Ignored, Matching or Different can be set on every row but those | **done** (`kicad-port/findByProperties.ts` `buildRows`); a class is the registry's (`PCB_TRACK` and `PCB_ARC` are two) | `rebuildPropertyGrid`, `isVisibleInFindByProperties`, `formatFindByPropertiesDisplayValue` |
| A selection of footprints also has a row for each field they all have that the properties do not show (a user field's name and text) | **done** | `getCommonFootprintFieldNames`, `getFootprintFieldValue` |
| A Match mode other than Ignored is kept for the row of the same name when the grid is built again, unless that row is `<...>`; with nothing selected the label says so, the grid is empty, Create from Selection Tab is off and the rows are kept (KiCad returns before it clears them), so Select Matching can be pressed again after it cleared the selection | **done** | `rebuildPropertyGrid` (`savedModes`, the early return), `onGridCellChanged` |
| Select Matching on that tab: every item of the board (tracks and vias, footprints with their pads and fields, graphics, texts and dimensions, zones; not groups) whose class is one of the selection's and that is equal (Matching) or different (Different) to the row for each row that is not Ignored | **done** (`selectMatchingFromRows`) | `collectAllBoardItems`, `itemMatchesPropertyCriteria`, `selectMatchingFromProperties` |
| Deselect non-matching items (on) clears the selection first, Zoom to fit selected items (off) frames the matches (`zoomFitBox`: the fit of Zoom to Selection), and the label says "N items matched"; the grid is built for the new selection at once and keeps that label | **done** | `applyMatchResults` |
| Query: Recently Used (the last ten queries that compiled, newest first, once each; kept in the browser's local storage, KiCad keeps them in the project file), the expression, a status line, Check Syntax ("Syntax OK", "Syntax error: ...", "Expression is empty", "B. expressions are not supported."), and Create from Selection Tab, which writes the rows as `A.<Property_Name> == value` (a length as `0.25mm`, a choice or a text quoted, a flag as `true`/`false`, a field as `A.getField('Name')`) joined by `&&` and goes to this tab. Enter in the editor selects what matches (the editor is one line); a text that does not compile says "Syntax error in expression" and opens the Expression Error box with the compiler's messages; after `A.` the names of the properties and fields are offered | **done** (the offer is a row of buttons under the editor, not Scintilla's pop-up) | `onCheckSyntaxClick`, `onCreateQueryClick`, `selectMatchingFromQuery`, `generateExpressionFromProperties`, `formatValueForExpression`, `saveRecentQuery`, `onScintillaCharAdded` |
| The query is read as KiCad reads it: `A.Reference`, `A.Datasheet`, `A.Description` and a field's name (blanks as `_`) mean `A.getField('..')` outside quotes; any `B.` is refused; the text goes to `PCBEXPR_COMPILER`; an item matches when the result is not zero on any layer it is on | **done** | `normalizeQueryFieldAliases`, `queryUsesUnsupportedPairwiseSyntax`, `selectMatchingFromQuery` |
| The expression language (`kicad-port/libeval.ts`: the lexer, the grammar with its precedences, the code generator and the stack machine of `libeval_compiler`): only the space is white space; a unit (`mil mm in deg fs ps`, case sensitive) is read before an identifier; `1,5` is 1.5; a string takes `\'`; `||` binds tighter than `&&` (the order of the `%left` lines of `grammar.lemon`); the comparisons and `.` do not chain; `!` binds looser than `+`; unary minus is `0 - x`; strings compare without case and a literal with `?` or `*` is a pattern on the right of `==`; an undefined value is neither equal nor different to anything; a run that leaves the stack other than with one value is false; an unrecognized character, a syntax error, an unknown item, property or function and a single number with no unit say what KiCad says (`Unrecognized character '%c'`, `Syntax error`, `Unrecognized item 'X'`, `Unrecognized property 'p'`, `Unrecognized function 'f'`, `Missing units for '5'| (must be mm, in, mil, deg, fs, or ps)`); `Compile` is true for a text that parses, whatever the codegen told (so "Syntax OK" is said of an unknown property, as KiCad says it) | **done** | `COMPILER::lexDefault`, `Compile`, `generateUCode`, `UOP::Exec`, `VALUE::EqualTo`, `grammar.lemon` |
| The board's side of it (`kicad-port/pcbExpr.ts`): `A.<Property>` reads the Properties panel's own property of the item's class by name (case ignored, `_` for a blank; a pad reads its footprint's `Reference` or `Value` when it has none), as the number it is (a length in nanometres, so `0.25mm` is 250000; a flag 1 or 0), the label of a choice, or text (a text such as `0.2mm` is a number); `A.Type` (Footprint, Pad, Track, Via, Zone, Text, Graphic, Dimension, Leader, Group), `A.NetName`, `A.NetClass` (equal to any class of a composite one), `A.Layer` (equal to every layer whose canonical or board name a pattern matches), `L` the layer being tried; `A.existsOnLayer`, `isPlated`, `isBlindVia`, `isBuriedVia`, `isBlindBuriedVia`, `isMicroVia`, `memberOf`/`memberOfGroup`, `memberOfFootprint`, `getField`, `hasNetclass`, `hasExactNetclass`, `inDiffPair`, `isCoupledDiffPair` | **done** (the Type and Net Class properties are registered, hidden from the panel: `kicad-port/pcbProperties.ts`) | `PCBEXPR_UCODE::CreateVarRef`, `PCBEXPR_VAR_REF::GetValue`, `PCBEXPR_LAYER_VALUE`, `PCBEXPR_NETCLASS_VALUE`, `PCBEXPR_NET_VALUE`, `PCBEXPR_TYPE_REF`, `pcbexpr_functions.cpp` |

Tests (51 new `node --test` cases): `kicad-port/libeval.test.ts` (17: numbers and units, the decimal comma, white space, strings, an empty text, the precedence of `||` and `&&`, the operators that do not chain, `!` and unary minus, syntax errors, what the compiler tells, the missing-units message, the order of a call's arguments, value comparison, a result's unit, type mismatches, `WildCompareString`), `kicad-port/pcbExpr.test.ts` (14: `A.Type`, lengths in nanometres, property names and an unavailable property, a pad reading its footprint's `Reference`, flags and choices, a text that is a length, net names and classes, a differential pair, layers, vias, groups and fields, what the compiler tells, selecting) and `kicad-port/findByProperties.test.ts` (20: the rows of a track, mixed values, items of several classes, footprint fields, Match modes kept, Select Matching by rows, the generated query and that it selects the same, the aliases, `B.`, Check Syntax, the completion, recent queries).

Seen in the browser (port 8827, a scratch board made with `eda place examples/mcu_board_30plus.yaml` and `eda board new`): `e2e/search-inspect.check.js` runs six scenarios (49 checks) through `window.__eda`: the dialog opens and lists the footprint's properties in order with Type; Matching and Different select the footprints on one package and the label counts them, the rows are used again with nothing selected, Deselect non-matching items off adds to the selection; Zoom to fit selected items frames one footprint; Create from Selection Tab writes `A.Library_Link == '0402' && A.Value == '100nF'` and that query selects the same footprints; the Query tab's capacitors by reference, a length with its unit, a layer by its name, the pads on GND, a choice by its label, `getField`, `||` before `&&`, a query that does not compile and its box, Recently Used, an unknown property that compiles and selects nothing, the names offered after `A.`; and the board as it was, with no console error.

**Approximations, and what is left.**
- The geometry functions (`intersectsCourtyard`, `intersectsArea`, `enclosedByArea`, their front and back variants and the old `insideCourtyard`/`insideArea`) compile and say false here: they need the shapes of the items (the rule engine of section 32c, `crates/drc/src/pcbexpr.rs`, has them). `fromTo` (the from-to cache), `memberOfSheet` and `memberOfSheetOrChildren` (a footprint has no sheet path in the IR) and `hasComponentClass` (no component classes yet) say false too.
- `A.Net` is the net's name here (it is the net code in KiCad, so `A.Net == 'GND'`, which is what Create from Selection Tab writes for a Net row, matches nothing there); `true` and `false` are the numbers 1 and 0 (KiCad has neither, and its generated query for a flag calls them unrecognized items); a length written with a unit is rounded to a whole nanometre so `==` works on `0.07mm`; a property the panel says an item does not have (`available`) reads as undefined where KiCad's getter would answer with a number that means nothing.
- A footprint's graphics and its texts other than its fields are not items of the studio's selection (they are drawn with their footprint), so a query cannot select them. The studio has no `Parent` property.
- Recently Used lives in the browser's local storage, not in the project file.

### 32b. Find follows the IR (`dialog_find.cpp`, `pcb_field.cpp`, `pcb_text.cpp`, `zone.h`)

Find (Ctrl+F, F3, Shift+F3; section 7a) was written when a footprint had a reference and a value and a zone no name. The IR has since grown fields (`Part.fields`: the Reference and Value with their visibility, the user fields), a footprint's own texts (`Part.texts`) and `Zone.name`, and the search reads them now (`kicad-port/pcbFind.ts` `findHits`, `footprintHit`).

| Behavior | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| A footprint comes into the list when its Reference field matches (references on), else its Value field (values on), else one of its own texts (other text items on), else any of its fields (other text items on): so a user field's text finds the footprint, and with other text items on the Reference and Value fields do too whatever the references and values boxes say | **done** | `DIALOG_FIND::search` (the loop over `fp->GetFields()`) |
| Include hidden fields: a field with Show off is searched only with the box on, the Reference and Value included (a footprint whose reference is hidden is not found by it) | **done** (the box did nothing before) | `PCB_FIELD::Matches` (`!IsVisible() && !searchAllFields`) |
| A footprint's own texts are searched as KiCad stores them: the fab text that shows the reference is `${REFERENCE}` (and `${VALUE}`), other texts what they say | **done** | `PCB_TEXT::Matches`, `FOOTPRINT::GraphicalItems` |
| A zone, a rule area too, is found by its name after the board's texts (with other text items on); an unnamed zone has the empty name, so only a mask of `*` finds it; the zone is selected and the view brought to it | **done** | `ZONE::Matches` (`GetZoneName`), `PCB_SELECTION_TOOL::FindItem` |
| A text, a field and a footprint's value are `UnescapeString( GetText() )` for the search and for the text the dialog opens with (`{slash}` is `/`); the dialog also opens with the first line of a selected footprint field | **done** (`unescapeString`, `kicad-port/schNetName.ts`) | `PCB_TEXT::Matches`, `PCB_EDIT_FRAME::ShowFindDialog` (`PCB_FIELD_T`) |
| The dialog's "Show search panel" link (`ACTIONS::showSearch`: the docked Search panel) | **not ported**: the studio has no Search panel; `common.Interactive.search` still jumps to the Activity tab | `DIALOG_FIND::onShowSearchPanel` |

Tests: six more in `kicad-port/pcbFind.test.ts` (a user field, hidden fields and the Reference and Value of a hidden one, a footprint's own texts, zones by name in KiCad's order with the empty name and the options, `UnescapeString`, the preload). Seen in the browser (port 8827): `e2e/search-inspect.check.js` gives a footprint a user field and a hidden one and the board a named zone through `/api/cmd` and Finds each (the hidden one only with Include hidden fields on, the zone only with other text items on, Ctrl+F on a selected field opening with its text), then undoes the three steps.

**Left:** the order of the nets in the hit list is by name (KiCad walks the net list by net code, and starts with the empty net 0, which a mask of `*` matches); the Search panel link above.

### 32c. Clearance Resolution and Constraints Resolution (`board_inspection_tool.cpp`, `drc_engine.cpp`, `drc_rule_parser.cpp`, `pcbexpr_evaluator.cpp`, `dsnlexer.cpp`)

Inspect > Clearance Resolution and Constraints Resolution answer which rule wins for the items you point at, and show every rule that was looked at, in KiCad's words. Nothing is checked: the rules are read the way kicad-cli's DRC reads them, and the answer is what it would enforce.
The studio side is `kicad-port/inspectReport.ts` (the report lines), `kicad-port/inspectPick.ts` (the pick), `actions/inspectActions.ts`, `components/InspectReportDialog.tsx` and `state/inspectReports.ts`; the engine is Rust: `crates/drc/src/{libeval,pcbexpr,dru,constraints,rule_item,rule_board,inspect}.rs`
and the route `crates/cli/src/inspect_api.rs` (`POST /api/inspect/clearance` and `/constraints`, read-only, no kicad-cli).

| Behavior | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| Inspect > Clearance Resolution (`pcbnew.InspectionTool.InspectClearance`; the context menu offers it with two items selected): two items selected give the report; one selected is the first item and "Select second item for clearance resolution..." picks the second; none selected picks both ("Select first item for clearance resolution..."), the first kept highlighted while the second is picked and the selection restored; the same item twice is "Select two different items for clearance resolution." (the info bar: an error toast) | **done** | `InspectClearance`, `pickItemForInspection` |
| Inspect > Constraints Resolution (`InspectConstraints`): one item selected gives the report, none picks it ("Select item for constraints resolution..."), several are "Select a single item for a constraints resolution report." | **done** | `InspectConstraints` |
| What a click picks: the canvas's own guess, and a footprint under a click that is also on a pad, a via, a track or a zone gives way to it; a footprint whose pads are all one logical pad (one number) is that pad, a footprint with several pads is itself; a group is its first member, an empty group is "Cannot generate clearance report on empty group."; a track is a run of segments in KiCad and a polyline here, so the segment nearest the click is meant (the first one when the items come from a selection) | **done** | `filterCollectorForInspection`, `reportClearance` (`checkFootprint`, the group case) |
| The report: a notebook of pages, each the lines of an HTML report box (`<h7>`, lists, `<b>`, `<i>`, links, `&apos;` and the other `EscapeHTML` entities; the lines are read into elements and never given to the page as markup); a page for items that share several copper layers has a "Layer:" choice over the report of each, the active layer first; "Show design rules." (shown when the rules file did not compile) opens Board Setup on its Custom Rules page; the dialog is modeless, created once and only hidden | **done** | `DIALOG_BOOK_REPORTER`, `WX_HTML_REPORT_BOX`, `reportCompileError` |
| Clearance pages: the copper clearance of each shared copper layer ("Items belong to the same net. Min clearance is 0." for one net), "Zone" (the zone connection, then the thermal-relief gap, spoke width and minimum spokes, or the zone's own clearance with the physical clearances, for a zone and a pad of its net), "Diff Pair" (gap and maximum uncoupled length), the two silkscreen layers (with the tented-via note), the two courtyards, "Hole" (hole clearance and hole to hole), "Edge.Cuts Clearance" and "Margin Clearance", and "Physical Clearances" (layer by layer, with the physical hole clearance after them) | **done** | `reportClearance` |
| Constraints pages: Track Width, Via Diameter, Via Annular Width, Hole Size, Solder Mask, Solder Paste (absolute and relative), Text Size (height and thickness), Keepouts ("Item disallowed at current location." or "Item allowed at current location.") and Assertions (every assertion rule tried on the item) | **done**; Text Size only for a board text (a footprint field cannot be picked) | `InspectConstraints` |
| The evaluation is `DRC_ENGINE::EvalRules`: the implicit rules first, the `.kicad_dru` rules in file order after them, each match overwriting the fields it sets, an explicit rule's match ending the evaluation; then the local clearance of a zone, a pad's and a footprint's own override (before the rules, except the board minimum), the net tie (zero clearance between the items of a net-tie footprint), the "board minimum" clearance and the inherited settings of a pad's parent footprint (zone connection, thermal gap and spoke width, solder mask and paste); the report lines are KiCad's ("Checking netclass 'HV' clearance: 0.5000 mm.", "Constraint applied.", "Membership not satisfied; constraint ignored.", "Rule applied; overrides previous constraints.", "Rule layer 'B.Cu' not matched; rule ignored.", "Local override on ...", "Board minimum clearance: ...") with the names HTML-escaped as KiCad escapes them; a returned value also lists every rule considered (`considered`: the rule, whether it applied, the line that said why) | **done** (`constraints.rs::RuleEngine::eval_rules`, one function; `Report`) | `DRC_ENGINE::EvalRules`, `processConstraint`, `applyConstraint` |
| The implicit rules (`loadImplicitRules`): the board setup constraints (track width, connection width, hole size, annular width, via diameter, hole to hole; zone fill strategy; silk; silk text height and thickness; hole; edge; courtyard), micro-via defaults, the barcode default, a rule per net class and per composite class for each setting it has (clearance and track width sorted by their minimum, then the diff pair width and gap, the via and micro-via sizes, a narrower diff pair gap that overrides the clearance), the keepout areas (`A.intersectsArea('<id>')` with the layers of the area and its disallow flags) | **done** (tuning-profile rules: see below) | `loadImplicitRules`, `NETCLASS::GetHumanReadableName`, `NET_SETTINGS::addMissingDefaults` |
| The rules file is `DRC_RULES_PARSER` over `DSNLEXER`: the lexer (a comment is a line that starts with `#`, strings with their escapes, keywords case sensitive), `version`, `rule` with `condition`, `layer` (`outer`, `inner` or a layer name pattern), `severity` and `constraint` with `min` / `opt` / `max` as expressions in units (`0.2mm + 5mil`, `100ps`; a count has none), `disallow`, `zone_connection`, `min_resolved_spokes`, `assertion`; the deprecated `mechanical_clearance`, `mechanical_hole_clearance` and `hole`; the errors KiCad throws ("Missing units for '1'", "Rule already has a 'clearance' constraint.", "Mixed units for constraint values.", "Unrecognized item ...") and that the first error ends the parse, so a file with a mistake gives the implicit rules alone and the report says "Report incomplete: could not compile custom design rules."; a condition that parses but names an unknown item, property or function is an error too (`compileRules`) | **done**; the 38 `.kicad_dru` files of `qa/data/pcbnew` all parse | `DRC_RULES_PARSER::Parse`, `parseConstraint`, `parseValueWithUnits`, `DRC_ENGINE::compileRules` |
| The expression language in Rust (`libeval.rs`, `pcbexpr.rs`; the same port as the browser's `libeval.ts` and held to the same cases): the lexer and grammar, the code generator with the messages `Compile` reports, the stack machine, `A`, `B`, `AB` and `L`, `A.Type`, `A.NetName`, `A.NetClass` (equal to any class of a composite one), `A.Layer` (by canonical or board layer name), the properties the items have by name, and the functions `existsOnLayer`, `isPlated`, `isMicroVia`, `isBlindVia`, `isBuriedVia`, `isBlindBuriedVia`, `memberOfGroup`, `memberOfFootprint`, `isCoupledDiffPair`, `inDiffPair`, `getField`, `hasNetclass`, `hasExactNetclass`, the courtyard and area functions (and their old names); a rule's condition is tried with `A` and `B` and then with `B` and `A` | **done**; every property name KiCad has compiles (`known_props.rs`), the ones the IR does not model read as undefined | `PCBEXPR_COMPILER`, `PCBEXPR_UCODE`, `pcbexpr_functions.cpp` |
| Lengths in a report are in the frame's units (`StringFromValue`, trailing zeros removed) and the engine's own lines in millimetres (`MessageTextFromValue`: `%.4f`, scientific notation when that would show only zeros); a track's description has its length in the frame's units | **done** | `EDA_UNIT_UTILS::UI::StringFromValue`, `MessageTextFromValue` |

Tests: 45 Rust cases and 8 `node --test` cases. `crates/drc` (`libeval::tests` 8: the lexer and grammar with KiCad's quirks, a comma between two numbers being one decimal; `pcbexpr::tests` 10: types, lengths, a pad reading its footprint's reference, nets and composite classes, layers by pattern and by both names, vias, what the compiler tells, the stack's quirks, numbers with units, `MatchDpSuffix` and wildcards; `dru::tests` 6: a rule, expressions as values, the three special constraints, the error messages (the first one ends the parse), a condition the compiler refuses, and all 38 rule files of `qa/data/pcbnew`; `constraints::tests` 15 new: the net class winner and the list of rules considered, a file rule beating the implicit ones and the later of two winning, a layer-scoped rule, a pad's own clearance and the board minimum, a zone's own clearance, a net tie, the width floor and the class optimum, a keepout area, an assertion, a bad rules file, the formats, and four rule sets of KiCad's own QA data: `issue16566` (an oval pad's 5 mm), `issue6879` (hole rules by pad type and the deprecated `hole`), `multinetclasses_drc` (`A.NetClass == 'Default'` is true of "CLASS1,Default" through its constituents, and the later rule wins) and `issue11814`);
`crates/cli/src/inspect_api.rs` 6: the winner and the rules considered, an explicit rule and a bad rules file, the pages that come with the items, a pad's constraints and its assertions, a track's nearest segment with the report in mils and a via, and **the proof against kicad-cli** (`EDA_SLOW_TESTS=1`: for a net class, an explicit rule and a loosening rule the clearance the report resolves is the one kicad-cli's DRC enforces, a violation 10 um under it and none 10 um over; 27 s); `kicad-port/inspectReport.test.ts` 6 (the entities, the header, bold and the link, an unknown tag never becomes markup, the layer choice, the text of a report) and `inspectPick.test.ts` 2.
Seen in the browser (port 8827, a scratch copy of `mcu30`): `e2e/search-inspect.check.js` selects two pads and runs Clearance Resolution (the F.Cu page, both pads with their net and net class, "Checking netclass 'Default' clearance: 0.2000 mm.", the board minimum line, "Resolved min clearance: 0.2 mm.", the Physical Clearances page layer by layer), sets a rule through `/api/cmd` and sees "Rule applied; overrides previous constraints." and 0.77 mm, then a rules file that does not compile and the notice with its link, then undoes them; the picker prompts of both commands and what they refuse; Constraints Resolution of a pad (Solder Mask, Solder Paste, Keepouts, Assertions with "--> Assertion failed. <--"); and two real clicks on two pads of U1 opened the report with those pads; a real click on the second segment of a three-point GND track (added through `/api/cmd`, then undone) opened the Constraints report of "Track [GND] on F.Cu, length 6.0000 mm" with its Track Width, Solder Mask, Keepouts and Assertions pages, so the click point survives the trip from the canvas to the nearest segment.

**Approximations, and what is left.**
- The implicit rules of the tuning profiles (`addTuningSingleRule`, `addTuningDifferentialRules`: the width and gap a profile asks of its net classes) are not built; the profiles are in the IR since P9a.
- A footprint has no sheet path or component class in the IR (`memberOfSheet`, `memberOfSheetOrChildren` and `hasComponentClass` say false), `fromTo` says false, and `getField` knows a footprint's Reference, Value and Footprint only; a rule that names a property KiCad has and the IR does not compiles and reads it as undefined (so `A.Teardrop` conditions never match).
- A via is through, blind or buried by its layers (the IR has no micro via type, so `A.Via_Type == 'Micro'` never matches); a pad's solder paste margin and a footprint's are not in the IR; the net-tie cache covers the pads of a net-tie group (a footprint's copper graphics that touch them are not given the nets).
- The geometry functions use the shapes of the items and treat touching as intersecting (KiCad deflates the area by an epsilon); `enclosedByArea` is decided on the vertices and the area's edges.
- Text variables and cross references in the rules text are not expanded (`${VAR}` is an unresolved variable and a parse error here; KiCad expands the project's and the board's before it reads the file).
- A footprint's field (`REF:Name`) and a text box cannot be picked; the courtyard notice of the Constraints report ("some footprint courtyards are malformed") is never shown.
- Item descriptions come from the studio's own items (`Pad 1 [GND] of R1 on F.Cu`, `Track [GND] on F.Cu, length 4.0000 mm`, `Zone 'name' [GND] on F.Cu, priority 0`), as `GetItemDescription` words them; the layer names are the board's.


## 33. Design Rules Checker: Delete Marker, Delete All Markers, a click on a marker, Inspect and Fix, Exclude all violations of rule, Save... (GAPS.md item 11, package P15; 2026-10-14)

Ported from `pcbnew/dialogs/dialog_drc.cpp` (`OnDeleteOneClick`, `OnDeleteAllClick`, `deleteAllMarkers`, `OnRunDRCClick`, `OnDRCItemRClick`, `OnSaveReport`), `common/rc_item.cpp` (`RC_TREE_MODEL::DeleteCurrentItem`, `DeleteItems`),
`pcbnew/drc/drc_item.cpp` (`DRC_ITEMS_PROVIDER::DeleteItem`, `DRC_ITEM::GetViolatingRule`), `pcbnew/board.cpp` (`RecordDRCExclusions`, `DeleteMARKERs`), `pcbnew/tools/board_inspection_tool.cpp` (`InspectDRCErrorMenuText`, `InspectDRCError`),
`pcbnew/tools/drc_tool.cpp` (`FixDRCErrorMenuText`, `FixDRCError`, `CrossProbe`) and `pcbnew/tools/pcb_selection_tool.cpp` (`selectPoint` on a `PCB_MARKER`). The pure rules are `kicad-port/rcItems.ts` (`patchDrcDeleteMarker`,
`patchDrcDeleteAll`, `violatingRule`, `drcInspectEntry`, `drcFixEntry`, node tests in `rcItems.test.ts`), what a click or an entry does is `actions/checkerOps.ts`, the dialog `DrcDialog.tsx`, the canvas `Canvas.tsx`.

| Behaviour | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| Delete Marker (a button of the dialog's footer, on when a marker of the page that is up is selected; the three pages) | **identical** | the marker leaves the list and the canvas, the row that followed it is selected (`RC_TREE_MODEL::DeleteCurrentItem( true )`, `DRC_ITEMS_PROVIDER::DeleteItem( aDeep )` = `BOARD::Delete`). Nothing is fixed by it: the next Run DRC finds the violation again |
| ... of an excluded marker | **identical**, undoable | the exclusion goes with it: `BOARD::RecordDRCExclusions`, which `OnRunDRCClick` calls before every run and the project save calls again, keeps the exclusions of the markers that are on the board, so a marker that was deleted is no longer excluded at the next run. `delete_drc_exclusions`, one undo step; the report on screen drops the row at once |
| Delete All Markers | **identical** | every marker of every page; with exclusions in the report KiCad asks "Delete exclusions too?" (`OnDeleteAllClick`) and so does the dialog, with "Errors and Warnings Only", "Errors, Warnings and Exclusions" and Cancel; the lists then say "All markers were deleted. Run DRC to check the board again." (`m_drcRun = false`) |
| ... with the exclusions | **identical**, undoable | no marker left, so `RecordDRCExclusions` keeps no exclusion: `Cmd::ClearDrcExclusions` (`review::clear_drc_exclusions`) forgets the whole list as one step |
| A left click on a marker on the board | **identical in spirit** | `PCB_SELECTION_TOOL::selectPoint` takes a `PCB_MARKER` like any item, `DRC_TOOL::CrossProbe` selects its row in the dialog when that is up: the marker is the selection (the items that were selected are not), it is drawn selected and the dialog shows it. Only a marker that is drawn counts (its kind is switched on in the Objects tab); Alt+click still lists everything under the pointer |
| A double click on a marker | **identical in spirit** | `PCB_EDIT_FRAME::OnEditItemRequest`: the dialog comes up with the marker's row selected |
| Exclude all violations of rule '...' / Remove all exclusions for violations of rule '...' | **identical for the rules a report names**, undoable | `OnDRCItemRClick` (`ID_ADD_EXCLUSION_ALL`) over `DRC_ITEM::GetViolatingRule`: a custom rule that is not implicit. kicad-cli's report has no field for it but its text says it (`DRC_CONSTRAINT::GetName` is `rule 'name'`), so the entry is offered for a violation whose text names a rule and works on the violations of every page that name it (`violatingRule`); the entry for the whole check stays beside it |
| Inspect entries of the marker menu | **identical** | `InspectDRCErrorMenuText`: Clearance Resolution (clearance, copper_edge_clearance, hole_clearance, hole_to_hole, starved_thermal), Constraints Resolution (text_height, text_thickness, diff_pair_uncoupled_length_too_long, track_width, track_angle, track_segment_length, via_diameter, annular_width, drill_out_of_range, microvia_drill_out_of_range, connection_width, assertion_failure), Compare Footprint with Library (lib_footprint_mismatch): the report of the violation's items from `GET /api/inspect/*` (section 32), or the footprint comparison, for the items it names |
| Fix entries of the marker menu | **identical** | `FixDRCErrorMenuText`, `FixDRCError`: Manage Footprint Libraries... (lib_footprint_issues), Update Footprint... (lib_footprint_mismatch), Change Footprint... (footprint_filters_mismatch), Update PCB from Schematic... (the parity checks: footprint_symbol_mismatch and its field twin, missing_footprint, duplicate_footprints, extra_footprint), Edit Footprint Properties... (footprint_type_mismatch, footprint), Edit Pad Properties... (padstack, padstack_invalid), Edit Text / Field / Dimension Properties... (text_height, text_thickness, mirrored_text_on_front_layer, nonmirrored_text_on_back_layer: by what the main item is), Cleanup Tracks & Vias... (track_dangling, via_dangling). The action runs on the violation's main item, selected first when the action works on the selection (`selectThenRun`) |
| Save... (a button of the footer: Report file (*.rpt), JSON file (*.json)) | **identical, by running KiCad's own** | `OnSaveReport` over `DRC_REPORT::WriteTextReport` / `WriteJsonReport`: `POST /api/drc/report` runs `kicad-cli pcb drc --format report|json --units <the editor's> --severity-*` for the Show boxes (`RC_ITEMS_PROVIDER::GetSeverities`), with the dialog's Refill zones and parity boxes, on the derived board and project (`eda_kicad_engine::cli_command`); the text goes to the browser as `DRC.rpt` / `DRC.json`, as a download (no file dialog). A fresh run: a marker deleted from the list is in it |
| Exclusions reach kicad-cli | **partial, and why** (as in section 9a, the written form changed) | `board.design_settings.drc_exclusions` holds `kiapi.board.DrcExclusion` records -- the kind of finding (`DRCET_*`), the marker's position in nanometres, the uuids of its items, the comment -- which is what kicad-cli 10.99.0-5334 keeps in a project (`crates/kicad/src/drc_exclusions.rs`); the pipe-delimited text of `PCB_MARKER::SerializeToString` is what it migrates a section at schema 2 or less from, and drops from any other, so none is written. The section says it is at schema 3 (`board.design_settings.meta.version`). The marker's position stays the catch of section 9a |

Not done: "Report all errors for each track" (`--all-track-errors` is not asked for) and the cross-probe options; a marker of the kinds that carry a layer (`copper_sliver`, `starved_thermal`, `generic-*`, `unresolved_variable`) is waived in the studio's report alone, as the report does not say its layer; the page saves a report as a download, it has no file dialog.

Tests: `rcItems.test.ts` (the pure rules, and that every check the Inspect and Fix tables name is a key KiCad reports), `crates/ops/src/review_tests.rs` (`ClearDrcExclusions`), `crates/cli/src/review_tests.rs` (Delete Marker and Delete All Markers are undoable steps and the project follows).
kicad-cli proofs, slow tier, on kicad-cli 10.99.0-5334: `a_waived_violation_reaches_kicad_cli_through_the_derived_project_and_kicad_cli_stops_reporting_it` (`board.rs`: the records make kicad-cli list the via and the track as excluded, with the comment, and leave them out of a plain
report; Delete All Markers' `ClearDrcExclusions` makes it report the track again, Undo brings the waivers back).
Verified in the browser (port 8830, a pristine placed copy of `examples/mcu_board_30plus.yaml`, 2026-10-15): `e2e/review.check.js` is a 91-step check through `window.__eda` that passes. Its board half: Delete Marker (off until a row is selected, the
counts follow, the row that followed is selected, Run DRC finds the violation again), a left click on a marker on the board (the row is selected and the items that were selected are not), Exclude with comment and then Delete Marker on the excluded marker (the
design's exclusion goes with it, Undo brings it back), Delete All Markers (the question, Errors and Warnings Only, Errors, Warnings and Exclusions, Undo), the Inspect entry (Clearance Resolution opens the Clearance Report), the Fix entry (Cleanup Tracks & Vias...
opens that dialog), a plain violation's menu without either, "Exclude all violations of rule 'silk_apart'" on a custom rule and its Remove twin, and Save... (`DRC.rpt`, `DRC.json`, with a Show box off). Seen by eye: the dialog's footer (Delete Marker, Delete All
Markers, Save..., Close) and the Schematic Setup page of section 28.

## 34. Design blocks on the board: the pane, Place Design Block, Save Board and Selection as Design Block (`pcb_design_block_control.cpp`, `pcb_design_block_utils.cpp`, `pcb_control.cpp::AppendDesignBlock`, `PlaceLinkedDesignBlock`, `SaveToLinkedDesignBlock`, `AppendBoard`, `pcb_group.cpp`, `pcb_io_kicad_sexpr.cpp`; GAPS.md item 32; P18)

Added 2026-10-20 (`task-design-blocks`; the library, its table and the schematic's half are `PARITY-sch.md` section 33). The board editor's Design Blocks pane is the schematic's with the boxes Place repeated copies and Place as group only (the board's Keep annotations box is not there: `AppendBoard` never reads it) and kicad-cli's drawing of the block's layout
(`pcb export svg --mode-single`) as the preview. A block's board is a `.kicad_pcb` in its folder; a group keeps its block (`Group.design_block`, `(lib_id "Lib:Name")` in the group of the `.kicad_pcb`).

| Behaviour | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| View > Design Blocks, the tree, the search, the menu | **identical in effect**, the outermost docked pane on the right: as the schematic's (`PARITY-sch.md` section 33); the menu has Save Board as Design Block..., Update Design Block from Board | `PCB_DESIGN_BLOCK_PANE`, `PCB_DESIGN_BLOCK_CONTROL::Init` |
| Place Design Block (Shift+B, a double click) | **identical in effect**, one undo step per copy: the block's board is put on the board with its origin at the cursor and carried by the Move tool until a click drops it (Escape takes it back), as Append Board does; the references the file has are kept unless the board uses them (the paste numbers those anew); Place as group puts what came in in a group named after the block and linked to it when two or more items came; with Place repeated copies the next copy is carried when one is dropped and Escape ends it. "Design block has no layout to place." / "Could not find design block %s." | `PCB_CONTROL::AppendDesignBlock` (`do { AppendBoard } while( repeatPlacement )`), `AppendBoard`'s `placeAsGroup` |
| Place Linked Design Block / Save to Linked Design Block | **identical in effect**: from one selected linked group: the block is placed again once (no repeat loop), or the block's layout is updated from the group's items; "Design block %s does not have a board file." | `PCB_CONTROL::PlaceLinkedDesignBlock`, `SaveToLinkedDesignBlock` |
| Save Board as Design Block... | **identical in effect**: Design Block Properties opens with the board's name; a name that exists asks to overwrite; the block's layout is the board (`export_kicad_pcb_with`) | `PCB_EDIT_FRAME::SaveBoardAsDesignBlock` |
| Save Selection as Design Block... | **identical in effect**, the link is one undo step: the layout is the selection's items (a lone group stripped to its members) with the nets they use, as a real board file; afterwards the items are in a group named after the block and linked to it (or the lone group takes the link), and that group is selected | `PCB_EDIT_FRAME::SaveSelectionAsDesignBlock`, `saveSelectionToDesignBlock` |
| Update Design Block from Board / from Selection | **identical in effect**: "Design block '%s' already has a layout." / "Overwrite existing layout?"; no dialog (the metadata stays) | `UpdateDesignBlockFromBoard`, `UpdateDesignBlockFromSelection`, `checkOverwriteDbLayout` |
| Properties..., Delete, the group's Library link | **identical**: as the schematic's; Group Properties on the board has the box too (`edit_group`'s `design_block`) | `DESIGN_BLOCK_PANE`, `DIALOG_GROUP_PROPERTIES` |
| Apply Design Block Layout | **not done**: repeats the block's layout on the footprints of a group with `MULTICHANNEL_TOOL::RepeatLayout` (rule areas built from the two hulls); the studio has no multichannel tool. Its row stays in `ui-parity-missing.json` | `PCB_CONTROL::ApplyDesignBlockLayout` |
| Not done | | Save Board as Design Block refuses a board with no footprint, track or graphic ("The board has nothing in it to save as a design block."; KiCad would write an empty file); Append Board still does not group what it appends (KiCad does by default: `place_as_group`, named after the file); a locked item in the block locks the group in KiCad (`SetLocked`) and not here; the other limits of `PARITY-sch.md` section 33 |

Tests: `crates/ops/src/design_blocks_tests.rs` (3: a block placed as a group is one linked group named after the block and a repeated copy is named, no group when not asked or for one item and a bad block is refused, the link verb, Group Properties' link and the link surviving Duplicate), `crates/cli/src/design_block_tests.rs` (a board saved as a block is placed as a linked group in one undo step; a selection of the board is grouped and linked in one undo step and Update writes over its layout; Properties and Delete; a read only library is not written), `crates/kicad/tests/sch_groups.rs` (`(lib_id ...)` of a board group), `kicad-port/designBlocks.test.ts`.
kicad-cli proofs, slow tier, on kicad-cli 10.99.0-5334 (`design_block_tests.rs`): the board blocks this studio writes (the whole board, a selection) load in kicad-cli (`pcb drc`, `pcb export svg`) and a block made from KiCad's own `Arduino_Nano` template places on the studio's board with its six footprints on the template's nets, grouped and linked.
Verified in the browser (port 8836, a pristine copy of `examples/mcu_board_30plus.yaml` with three parts placed, 2026-10-20): `e2e/design-blocks.check.js` (106 steps, passes). Its board half: the pane has no Place as sheet box, Save Board as Design Block (three footprints in the file), Save Selection (a group linked to the block, selected), Place Design Block carried and dropped (three footprints and the group, one undo step takes them away), repeated copies (the next copy is carried after each drop, Escape takes the last back), Place Linked, Save to Linked ("Overwrite existing layout?"), Delete, and the board as it was after the clean-up.
